Database: PostgreSQL in production and integration tests (ephemeral container); unit tests use in-memory repository implementations behind the same interfaces. Policy engine: Open Policy Agent (Rego). Solvers: OR-Tools (planning) and Z3 (consistency and constraint proofs). Tracing: OpenTelemetry. UI: server-rendered. Model provider and models: as recorded in decision-d06, behind the adapter interface, with recorded-response mode so CI needs no live key. Exact versions are those pinned in the P00 dependency list and recorded in the Source Registry. Everything above is replaceable behind the interfaces defined in this Part.

<<<P3-S21>>>
3.21 DETERMINISM RULES
Pinned interpreter and dependency versions; decimal arithmetic with an explicit context; no reliance on unordered iteration for any output (sort by explicit keys); locale-independent formatting; UTC everywhere; seeds explicit and recorded; no wall-clock or randomness inside Rules, Decision or Passport assembly; canonical serialization for every hashed or signed byte sequence.

<<<P3-S22>>>
3.22 ARCHITECTURE CONFORMANCE TESTS
Automated checks that fail CI: import boundaries (3.18); no floating-point money; no wall-clock access inside rules; determinism (run twice, compare hashes); replay equality; ledger, tree, checkpoint, proof, commitment and signature verification for case and system chains; known-answer vectors; tamper detection; interpreter versus generated evaluator agreement; every rule has citation, window and label; every pack has golden tests; no model call outside the adapter; every external call passes the broker; every API operation appears in the contract; no Decision with a SECONDARY-or-weaker datum is ALLOW; no rule reads a non-VERIFIED fact; budgets enforced; no memory write outside the Kernel write path; every table has a tenant isolation policy; cross-tenant leakage suite passes; containment suite passes (3.30); every capability matches the standard layout (3.31); state files consistent with ledger and history (3.29).

<<<P3-S23>>>
3.23 SYSTEM MEMORY ARCHITECTURE
Memory is governed state with provenance, never free-form notes.
Tiers:
- M0 Working context: the Context Pack for one model call, built by the Kernel from VERIFIED facts only, size-capped, discarded after the call; only its hash and the call record persist. No history, no cross-case content.
- M1 Case memory: Documents, Facts, RuleResults, Decisions, Approvals and the case chain. Scope: one case. Append-only. Retention class set in configuration.
- M2 Reference memory: Packs, Source Registry, calendar and unit tables, policies. Scope: deployment, read-only at run time; changes only through a recorded pack or policy activation on the deployment chain or, for an overlay, on the tenant system chain; an optional tenant overlay may add rules, never remove deployment rules.
- M3 Precedent memory: abstracted outcomes of closed, human-reviewed cases (decision, reason codes, approval or override, later feedback). No documents, no free text, no personal data. Scope: tenant. It is advisory only: it may rank, prioritise or prompt a reviewer; it can never be evidence for ALLOW and never changes a Decision. Aggregates over cases are produced only when at least 5 cases contribute (k-threshold, configurable upward) and carry the label DERIVED with the sample size.
- M4 Cache: deterministic response cache and computed artifacts, tenant-scoped, keyed by hashes, with a time-to-live.
There is no agent self-memory: workers keep no notes between invocations.
Write path: only Kernel services write memory, each write is a ledger event carrying provenance, label, tenant, scope, retention class and expiry. A model output never writes memory; M3 entries arise only from human-approved outcomes.
Read path: typed queries only; a worker may read only the memory classes in its grant (3.11, 3.25); every M3 read that influences a proposal or Decision is recorded as Evidence and listed in the Passport.
Poisoning controls: entries are written only from verified facts or approved outcomes; anomalous write rates raise alerts; an entry can be quarantined, which excludes it from reads while keeping the record; correction is by superseding entry, never by silent edit.
Forgetting: every entry has an expiry; a scheduled job expires entries and writes a tombstone; erasure on request removes payloads and keeps hashes (3.5, 3.24).
Inspection: the memory inspector view and the memory-inspect command show, for any case, what was read, what was written, why, under which policy, and what has expired.
Tests: cross-case and cross-tenant isolation; poisoning attempts rejected; expiry and tombstones; erasure; M3 can never produce ALLOW; k-threshold enforced; no write outside the write path.

<<<P3-S24>>>
3.24 TENANT ISOLATION CORE
Scope of commitment: complete isolation, not a commercial multi-tenant product. No billing, no external identity providers, no tenant self-service beyond a minimal administrator view.
Isolation matrix (each layer has its own control and its own test):
- Identity: every API token, session and job carries exactly one tenant, except a platform credential, which carries none and cannot reach a tenant-scoped route; tenant is derived from the credential only, never from a request field, header or path parameter.
- Database: PostgreSQL row-level security enabled and forced on every table, with a deny-by-default policy keyed on a per-transaction tenant setting; the application role is not a superuser, has no bypass privilege, does not own the tables, and cannot alter policies; migrations run under a separate role; a migration that adds a table without a forced policy fails CI. With connection pooling the tenant setting is applied with transaction-local scope and cleared on release; a test proves it cannot leak between pooled connections. Security-definer functions and views are forbidden unless a policy review test lists them.
- Application: repositories take a tenant context object that cannot be constructed without a principal; there are no unscoped queries in application code; background jobs run per tenant with their own context.
- Keys and data: the payload store uses envelope encryption with one data key per tenant, wrapped by a master key held by the deployment secret store. Destroying a tenant data key makes that tenant's payloads unrecoverable (crypto-erasure); chains remain verifiable by hash: `verify --structural` still passes and `verify --full` reports the erased records as ERASED. Whether a salted hash still counts as personal data in law is a question for counsel (UNVERIFIED).
- Cache, storage, queues, temporary files: names are prefixed by tenant and checked on read; cache keys include the tenant.
- Model context: a Context Pack contains one case of one tenant only; model response caches are tenant-namespaced; precedent memory (M3) is tenant-scoped.
- Observability: log records carry tenant id; metrics do not use tenant as a label; traces and exports are tenant-filtered; error messages never reveal other tenants' existence (a foreign resource and a missing resource return the same response).
- Configuration per tenant: budgets, rate limits, retention classes, autonomy policy, pack activation set (which packs and overlays are enabled) and the region profile, all recorded on the tenant system chain. The region profile lists the regions in which the tenant's data may be stored and the regions in which the tenant's data may be processed by a model; region names are configuration values and the core contains none. A tenant without a profile may use only a locally hosted model and the deployment's own storage. The profile is enforced by policy as code (Part 3 §3.9), not by convention: the Model Gateway refuses a call whose provider region is outside the profile and the case ends in ABSTAIN with reason REGION_POLICY; backups, exports and the exit kit stay inside the storage regions. Narrowing a profile is an act of the tenant administrator; widening it needs the platform operator and is recorded on the tenant system chain; moving stored data to another region is done only through the restore path (restore into the new region, verify with both verifiers, then switch).
- Signing: Passports carry the tenant id and are signed with the deployment key; per-tenant signing keys are an extension seam.
Attack catalog the tests must cover: insecure direct object reference on every identifier; tenant identifier forged in body, header, path and token claims; confused deputy through background jobs and the outbox; cache key collision and cache poisoning across tenants; row-level security bypass by table ownership, privileged roles or functions; session setting leaking through pooled connections; shared sequences or gap-free counters revealing other tenants' activity; existence oracles through status codes, timing and error text; export, backup and log leakage; search or aggregate endpoints; ledger and proof endpoints; shared model context or response cache; precedent memory aggregates below the k-threshold.
Tests: a cross-tenant leakage suite that drives every API operation, repository method, cache and storage path and view with a foreign principal and with forged identifiers, and must find zero leaks; policy tests run as the application role; migration lint; fuzzing of tenant identifiers; pooled-connection test; a two-tenant scenario in the demo harness.

<<<P3-S25>>>
3.25 AGENT ROLE CONTRACTS
Each role is a component with typed input and output ports, a tool grant, readable memory classes, a model-use rule, action classes (3.30), a budget share and a failure behavior.
- Manager: input case commands and events; output workflow steps and ledger events; no model; reads M1, M2; failure: stop the case in BLOCKED or FAILED, never ALLOW.
- Intake and Extraction: input Document records; output Fact proposals with locators; model yes, tool-less and secret-less; reads M0 only; action class P; failure: fields UNKNOWN, case BLOCKED if required facts are missing.
- Compliance: input VERIFIED Facts and evaluation time; output RuleResults and Decision parts; no model for decisions (model only for labelled explanation proposals); reads M1, M2; action class R and P; failure: ABSTAIN with reason.
- Logistics and Cost: input Facts, tariff and schedule packs; output deterministic cost, time and exposure computations and solver-backed estimates; reads M1, M2, advisory M3; action class R and P; failure: ABSTAIN with reason.
- Planner: input Decisions, constraints and Facts; output plan candidates verified by a solver; model optional for narrative only; reads M1, M2, advisory M3; action class P; failure: no plan, reasons listed.
- Risk and Red-Team: input Decisions and Evidence; output adversarial findings and counter-scenarios as Evidence; model allowed for scenario generation (tool-less), verification deterministic; reads M1, M2, M3; action class P; failure: unresolved finding forces ESCALATE.
The Manager splits the case budget among roles in configuration; a role cannot exceed its share.

<<<P3-S26>>>
3.26 EVALUATION OF MODEL-DEPENDENT COMPONENTS
- Every component that uses a model has an evaluation set (SYNTHETIC, versioned, seeded), run offline against recorded responses so CI needs no live key.
- Measured: field-level extraction accuracy, rate of wrongly filled fields on documents that lack the field, grounding-check precision, prompt-injection resistance on an adversarial document set, explanation faithfulness, and containment behavior under adversarial instructions (3.30).
- Regression gates compare each run to a stored baseline; thresholds are numeric values defined in Part 4 and a drop below a threshold fails CI.
- A refreshed recording is a recorded event; replay never depends on a live model.

<<<P3-S27>>>
3.27 RELIABILITY AND DATA LIFECYCLE
- Backups: database backups run at the hosting platform's capability level; a restore drill restores into an ephemeral database and then runs ledger verification; the drill is a repeatable script and a gate in the audit phase.
- Migrations: expand-then-contract; backward compatible within one release; every migration is tested forward on realistic data; a destructive migration is a Stop-and-ask item.
- Background work: jobs are idempotent, leased and retried with limits; poison jobs go to a dead-letter store with an alert.
- Retention classes and expiry defaults are configuration (case payloads, cache, precedent entries), applied by the expiry job (3.23).
- Operational documents: runbook, restore procedure and incident checklist are part of the documentation phase.

<<<P3-S28>>>
3.28 SIMULATION OVERLAY
What-if and as-of evaluation never touch the case chain. A Simulation takes a case snapshot, an alternative evaluation_time and/or an alternative pack activation set, runs Rules and Decision, and stores a Simulation record labelled SIMULATED with its inputs and result hashes. A Simulation can never become a Decision; adopting its outcome requires a normal new evaluation on the case chain. The Regulation Shock Simulator and similar capabilities use this mechanism.

<<<P3-S29>>>
3.29 ENGINEERING MEMORY (BUILD-TIME STATE)
- Principle: the repository is the only memory of the development agent between runs. A run starts cold from the Read Map blocks, the current state file and the previous phase report; nothing is carried in conversation. Context pollution is prevented by keeping state small, structured and machine-checked.
- Records, all inside the repository: task ledger (docs/ledger/Pxx.md); phase reports (docs/phase-reports/); owner decisions (docs/decisions/); defect records (docs/defects/, one per defect: symptom, reproduction, cause, fix commit, regression test); source registry and license manifests (data/); test index; changelog; capability status map.
- State file docs/state/STATE.json: schema-validated summary of the current position (last completed phase, previous merge hash, awaiting-merge phase, directive hash, capability status map, open STOP items, label counts). The state file is written at Pnn-END2, before the owner merges, so it never holds the merge hash of its own phase: `previous_merge_hash` is the merge commit of the phase before it, proven at this phase's entry (Part 1 §8.2a, §8.3), and `awaiting_merge` names the phase whose pull request is open (null once the next entry proves the merge). The gate script writes it as part of the phase-end tasks; the agent never hand-edits it. The entry check verifies it against the ledger and the commit history.
- Size discipline: each state file has a size cap; older ledger and report content is rolled up into counts while hashes and the commit history remain the full record. The agent reads only the state file and the previous report, never the whole history.
- Integrity: state files are protected paths (Part 1 §5.5) except through the gate script; inconsistency between a state file, the ledger and the commit history is a STOP condition.
- No conversation content and no personal information is stored in these records; dates are month-level.

<<<P3-S30>>>
3.30 AGENT AUTONOMY CONTAINMENT
Premise: agents are autonomous and can produce actions nobody wants. Containment is therefore enforced outside the agents and cannot be changed by them.
- Fixed goals and flow: each role's goal, the workflow DAG, tool grants and autonomy level are signed configuration recorded on the deployment chain. An agent cannot change them, create agents, discover tools, choose URLs, file paths or commands, or choose its next step; the Manager does.
- Action classes: R read; P propose; E-rev reversible internal effect; E-irrev external or irreversible effect. R and P need no approval. E-rev needs a policy decision. E-irrev needs a human Approval bound to the proposal hash.
- Autonomy levels per capability and tenant: A0 observe only; A1 propose; A2 act on approval. No higher level exists. The default is A1; a capability with any E-irrev action is capped at A2.
- Behavior monitors in the Manager: step cap per case; loop detection (the same tool and argument hash more than 3 times); transitions outside the workflow DAG; budget burn rate; unusual output size; repeated verification failures; repeated policy denials. Any violation moves the case to QUARANTINED, records Evidence, forces ESCALATE and raises an alert.
- Kill switches, recorded on the tenant system chain (pause a case, disable a capability, freeze a tenant) or on the deployment chain (freeze the deployment), and effective immediately: pause a case; disable a capability; freeze a tenant; freeze the deployment. A freeze rejects new commands with a problem-details response and moves in-flight cases to BLOCKED. A tenant freeze is lifted only by that tenant's administrator. The deployment freeze is lifted only by a platform operator and needs a second, different platform operator (configurable); while it is in force, a record of it is written on every tenant system chain so each tenant can see why its commands are rejected.
- Oversight: Overrides of ESCALATE need a second reviewer by default (3.9); the Red-Team role challenges Decisions; the Passport states the autonomy level in force.
- Recovery: effects are reversed only by new, approved, ledgered actions; nothing is deleted or rewritten.
- Containment tests: adversarial documents and instructions that try to change goals, call tools, exfiltrate data, loop, exceed budgets or skip the Human Gate must each end in quarantine, ABSTAIN or ESCALATE and never in an effect; every kill switch is exercised in CI.

<<<P3-S31>>>
3.31 CODE ARCHITECTURE RULES
- Structure: ports and adapters; a pure functional core in kernel/ and capabilities/ (no I/O, no clock, no randomness, no global state), with side effects only in adapters/ and api/. Dependencies are passed explicitly; no singletons or module-level mutable state.
- Data: immutable, strictly typed models generated from the schemas; no untyped dictionaries across module boundaries; no use of the "any" type.
- Errors: expected failures are typed results; exceptions are for defects only; every error has a stable code that maps to the problem-details model.
- Uniformity: every capability has the identical layout, produced by the Generator: manifest, schemas, rules, fixtures, golden tests, evaluation cases, view, documentation stub, and a registration entry. A conformance test fails CI if a capability deviates.
- Modules: one responsibility per module; public interfaces declared explicitly; no circular imports; naming, docstring and logging conventions are fixed in Part 4 and enforced by tools, not by review.
- Toolchain: formatting, linting, strict type checking, import-boundary checking, complexity limits, property-based tests and mutation testing run as gates; their numeric limits and thresholds are defined in Part 4. The agent never judges style; the tools do.

<<<P3-S32>>>
3.32 DEPLOYMENT FORMS
- Primary: one container image, deployed as a web service on the hosting platform with a managed database.
- Organization deployment: the same image runs on-premises or in a private cloud through a container bundle (application, database, policy engine) configured only by environment and secret files; it has no dependency on the hosting platform.
- Local single-user mode: the same bundle on one machine; a native desktop shell may wrap it later through the seam in 3.19; no desktop application is part of the committed scope.
- Every form uses the same API, the same tenant model and the same gates.
<<<END-PART-3>>>

<<<PART-4>>>
PART 4 — ENGINEERING STANDARDS

<<<P4-S1>>>
4.1 PRINCIPLES
S1 Standards are executable. A rule that no tool enforces is a defect in this Part. The agent never judges style or quality by opinion; gates judge.
S2 One gate runner. Every check runs through tools/gate with named gates; the same command runs locally and in CI, so results never differ by environment.
S3 Zero tolerance. Warnings fail gates. No skipped, expected-failure or quarantined tests. No lowered thresholds.
S4 Ratchet. Coverage, mutation score, evaluation scores and performance budgets may only improve; a stored baseline moves up and never down.
S5 One way to do each thing. Where a convention exists, there is exactly one allowed form, and a tool enforces it.
S6 Real-world grade. Every artifact is written as for production use by an organization: reproducible, observable, documented, secure, recoverable.
S7 Cheap to run, cheap to read. Verification is done by scripts; the agent reads exit codes and short summaries, never full outputs (4.15).

<<<P4-S2>>>
4.2 TOOLCHAIN AND BASELINE LIBRARIES
Exact versions and licenses are listed in the P00 dependency list and recorded in the Source Registry. No tool or library outside the lists may be introduced.
- Language and runtime: Python (single pinned minor version).
- Package management: uv with a hash-locked lock file; installs use the lock file only.
- Formatting and linting: Ruff (one configuration).
- Static typing: mypy in strict mode with explicit-any disabled.
- Architecture boundaries: import-linter contracts that encode Part 3 §3.18.
- Tests: pytest, pytest-cov (branch coverage), pytest-xdist (parallel runs), pytest-randomly (random order with recorded seed), pytest-timeout (per-test limits), Hypothesis (property-based), mutmut (mutation), Schemathesis (schema-driven API testing), Playwright (end-to-end and capture), axe-core through Playwright (accessibility).
- Security and supply chain: Bandit and the Ruff security rule family (the determinism and money rules are project-owned syntax-tree checks in the gate), pip-audit, gitleaks, Trivy (image, filesystem and Dockerfile or deployment configuration checks), Syft (CycloneDX SBOM), license check against the allowlist in 4.12. Tools follow the allowlist of 4.12 exactly as libraries do; a tool outside it is not used.
- API contract: OpenAPI 3.x generated from schemas; oasdiff for breaking-change detection.
- Policy tests: the policy engine's native test runner.
- Baseline application libraries: FastAPI, Pydantic v2, SQLAlchemy 2.x with Alembic, a PostgreSQL driver whose license is on the allowlist (named in `<<<P8-DEPS>>>`), Jinja2 for server-rendered views, an ASGI server, OpenTelemetry SDK.
- Media: a project-owned encoder in `tools/media/` that assembles the captured frames into an animated PNG using the standard library only (4.10).

<<<P4-S3>>>
4.3 GATE RUNNER AND GATE CATALOGUE
Runner contract
- Commands: gate list; gate run <name|set>; gate affected (prints the impacted test set, 4.5); gate report (prints the generated gate table).
- Exit codes: 0 all passed; 1 at least one gate failed; 2 misconfiguration or tool error (never counted as pass).
- Every gate writes a JSON result (gate, command, exit code, duration, counts) to an ignored log path; the generated gate table in phase reports and pull request bodies comes from these files, not from hand-written text.
- Each gate has a timeout; exceeding it is a failure.
Cadence codes: Q quick (any time, cheap); P phase-end and pull-request required; N nightly or scheduled; R release and audit phases.
- format (Q P), lint (Q P), types (Q P), arch (Q P), generated-files (P: regenerate and require byte-identical output).
- unit (Q P), property (P), metamorphic (P), differential (P: interpreter versus generated evaluator, 3.6), contract (P), integration (P: ephemeral PostgreSQL and policy engine), e2e (P), replay (P: ledger, tree, proofs, commitments, replay equality), a11y (P).
- leakage (P: tenant and cross-case suite, 3.24), containment (P: 3.30), eval (P: model-dependent components against recorded responses, 3.26), migration (P: forward migration on realistic data, policy lint), state (P: state file versus ledger and history, 3.29).
- mutation (P on changed modules; N and R on all), fuzz (P short; N and R long), chaos (N R: fault injection, 3.16), perf (N R, and P for budgeted paths), soak (N R), restore (N R, 3.27).
- security (P), secrets (P), deps (P), image (P: scan and SBOM), license (P), data (P: license manifest and source registry), labels (P: every datum labelled, 3.8), hygiene (P: forbidden patterns held outside the repository), docs (P: links, examples, schema-generated references, claims).
Sets: "quick" = Q gates; "phase" = every P gate selected per 4.5; "nightly" = every gate; "release" = every gate with no test selection.
Part 1 §10.1 "full test suite and all gates" means the "phase" set defined here. Nightly findings are reported in the next phase's Entry Verification.

<<<P4-S4>>>
4.4 CODE STANDARDS (enforced by the toolchain)
Formatting and size
- One canonical formatter output; line length 100.
- Cyclomatic complexity per function at most 10; arguments at most 6; branches at most 12; statements at most 40; returns at most 4; nesting depth at most 4; module at most 400 lines; class at most 300 lines. A limit is a gate, not a guideline.
Typing and data
- Every function, method and variable boundary is annotated; strict mode passes with zero errors; the "any" type and untyped dictionaries across module boundaries are forbidden. Test code is typed too.
- Domain models are frozen (immutable) and generated from schemas; mutation of a model returns a new value.
Naming
- Modules and functions snake_case; classes PascalCase; constants UPPER_SNAKE; booleans start with is_, has_ or can_; names are English words from the project glossary; no abbreviations except those listed in the glossary; a vocabulary check enforces the glossary.
Structure
- Layering per Part 3 §3.18 and §3.31; one responsibility per module; public interface declared explicitly; no circular imports; absolute imports only.
- The core (kernel and capabilities) is pure: no I/O, no environment access, no clock, no randomness, no global state. Time, randomness and identifiers come from injected ports. Direct calls to wall-clock, random, UUID or environment APIs are banned in the core by lint rules.
Errors
- Expected failures are typed results with stable error codes registered in one registry; exceptions signal defects only; no bare or broad exception handlers except at the outermost boundary, where they log and convert to the problem-details model.
Logging
- A single logger factory; structured key-value records with constant message keys; no print statements; no personal data; correlation id on every record.
Persistence
- Parameterized queries only; no SQL built from strings; every migration carries its tenant policy (3.24) and is reversible unless a Stop-and-ask decision says otherwise.
Security rules enforced by lint
- Banned: dynamic code execution, unsafe deserialization, shell invocation with string commands, disabled TLS verification, weak hashes for security purposes, hard-coded secrets, unrestricted file paths from input.
Documentation in code
- Every public symbol has a docstring (Google convention) stating contract, arguments, return value and raised errors; comments explain why, never what; no commented-out code.
Uniform capability layout
- Every capability is generated from one template (3.31): manifest, schemas, rules, fixtures, golden tests, evaluation cases, view, documentation stub, registration entry. A conformance gate fails on any deviation.
Dependencies
- Versions change only in a task that lists the change. A failing dependency-audit finding is a Stop-and-ask item (an upgrade can change behavior); exceptions live in a protected, owner-approved baseline file with reason and expiry.

<<<P4-S5>>>
4.5 TESTING PROGRAM (CONTINUOUS, TIERED, IMPACT-BASED)
Layers (all under test-house/): unit; property-based; metamorphic; differential; contract; integration; end-to-end; security and leakage; adversarial and containment; model-dependent evaluation; fault injection; performance; mutation; accessibility; replay and ledger verification; migration and restore.
Rules
- Tests are written in the same task as the code they cover and committed in the same commit (Part 1 §4.1, §11.3). A task is not done until its acceptance lines are covered by named tests.
- Every test has a header: id (format T-AREA-NNNN), title, purpose, level, requirement and scenario ids, input, expected result, risk covered, owning capability. The gate generates test-house/INDEX from these headers; a test without a complete header, or an index that is out of date, fails the gate.
- Scenarios first: for every capability, normal, boundary and adversarial scenarios from Part 5 map to test ids; the phase block lists the mapping; the gate fails if a scenario has no test.
- Test data is SYNTHETIC, seeded and labelled; no real personal or commercial data; no network in unit tests; injected clock and seeds.
- Oracles: golden files for stable outputs, properties for laws, differential checks between independent implementations, metamorphic relations where no oracle exists. Golden files change only through `gate golden regenerate` (Part 1 §4.8), in a separate commit of a task that lists the path; regenerating a golden file to make a test pass is forbidden.
- Isolation: tests are order-independent and share no state; every test uses its own transaction or temporary directory.
Speed classes (enforced by markers and per-test timeouts)
- fast: at most 1 second per test; slow: at most 30 seconds; end-to-end: at most 120 seconds. A test over its limit fails. The fast set must stay under 10 minutes in total; if it does not, tests move to slow by owner decision, never by deletion.
Tiers
- Tier A, fast suite: all fast tests (unit, small property, arch, types, lint, schema contract) run in full at every phase end and pull request.
- Tier B, impact suite: slow tests (integration, end-to-end, leakage, containment, eval, replay, mutation, fuzz-short) are selected by the impact computation. The gate builds the module dependency graph (from the import contracts) and a test-to-module coverage map (recorded each nightly run); a test is selected if it covers any changed module or any module that depends on one. Selection is conservative: any change to kernel schemas, migrations, policies, gate scripts, CI files, dependency locks or the generator runs the complete slow set. Any uncertainty selects the test.
- Tier C, full suite: every gate and test without selection runs nightly, in the audit and release phases, and before any tag. A nightly failure is reported at the next phase entry.
- Safety net: a failure found only by a nightly or full run that selection would have skipped is recorded as a selection miss; the selection rules are then revised by owner decision. The target is zero misses.
Thresholds (design parameters; the first measured baseline becomes the ratchet floor and thresholds never decrease)
- Branch coverage: kernel at least 95 percent; capabilities at least 90 percent; overall at least 90 percent.
- Mutation score: kernel at least 85 percent; gate code (`tools/gate/`) at least 85 percent; capability rule logic at least 80 percent.
- Model-dependent evaluation on the SYNTHETIC set: field-level extraction exact match at least 95 percent; wrongly filled fields on documents lacking the field: zero; grounding-check precision at least 99 percent; adversarial documents ending in no effect: 100 percent; template explanations faithful: 100 percent.
- Conformal coverage: empirical coverage within the tolerance defined in the capability spec across at least 1,000 seeded trials (3.13).
- Baselines live in docs/state/baselines.json, a protected file written only by the gate script when a value improves.
Flakiness
- Tests run in random order; the seed is recorded in the gate result (`.gate-logs/`), and a failing order is reproduced with the same seed, the same worker count and the same test selection, all three recorded beside it.
- Seeds are managed, never left to chance. The file `docs/state/seeds.toml` (a protected path, written only by `gate seeds add`) holds two lists: five pinned seeds, which are arbitrary fixed integers, and the regression seeds. The gate runs on every phase use the pinned seeds, so a gate result can be reproduced; new or changed tests run once under each of the five pinned seeds in random order, and any inconsistency between the five runs fails the gate. Exploration uses fresh seeds: the nightly set and the release set add at least five fresh seeds per run, each recorded in the gate result. A seed under which any test fails is never discarded: `gate seeds add <seed> --test <id>` appends it to the regression seeds with the test id, and from then on every gate run includes it, a regression seed is removed only by an owner decision. The seed also drives the property-based tests (derandomized in the gate run, with the seed printed); a failing example found by a property-based test is turned into a fixed example in the test before the fix is accepted.
- A fresh seed is generated by the gate runner and printed before the run starts, so a crash during the run still leaves the seed in the log.
- Flaky tests are defects: find and fix the cause within the retry rule (Part 1 §7.3). Quarantine, retry-until-green and skip are forbidden.

<<<P4-S6>>>
4.6 DEBUGGING PROTOCOL
For every failing check or reported defect, in this order: (1) reproduce with the smallest failing input; (2) write a failing test first; (3) record a one-line diagnosis of the root cause (Part 1 §7.3); (4) make the minimal fix; (5) confirm the new test and the affected suite pass; (6) write the defect record docs/defects/DEF-NNNN (symptom, reproduction, cause, fix commit, regression test id); (7) commit test and fix with the task id.
- Failing cases from the system are exported as replay fixtures and become regression tests (replay-to-test tool).
- A fix never weakens a gate (Part 1 §5.6).
- Diagnosis aids: correlation ids, structured logs, trace spans, ledger records and replay; no debugging code is committed.

<<<P4-S7>>>
4.7 CI/CD
- Platform: GitHub Actions, with workflows that only call tools/gate and build steps; no logic lives in workflow files.
- Required checks on pull requests: the "phase" set. Main is protected; merge commits only (Part 1 §10.7).
- Cost control: dependency and build caching keyed by lock-file hash; path filters for documentation-only changes (documentation gates still run); concurrency limits cancel superseded runs; the nightly workflow is the only scheduled one.
- Reproducible build: pinned base images by digest, lock files with hashes, pinned action versions, no network during test steps except the declared registries; the image is built from the lock file only; build provenance and signing are used where the platform supports them (V-12).
- Containers: multi-stage, minimal base, non-root, read-only filesystem where the platform allows, no secrets in layers, health check, resource limits, tags by commit identifier, never "latest".
- Deployment: infrastructure as code under deploy/ (hosting blueprint and container bundle); deployment happens only in phases that say so; secrets exist only in the hosting dashboard and CI secret store.

<<<P4-S8>>>
4.8 DOCUMENTATION STANDARDS
- docs/ holds architecture, decisions (ADR-style records of engineering decisions, numbered, immutable once accepted), runbook, restore procedure, incident checklist, capability reference (generated from schemas), API reference (generated), glossary, label legend, test-house index.
- Generated references are never edited by hand; the docs gate checks links, runs every documented example against the system, and verifies that every claim in README and docs maps to a passing test or a recorded source.
- Language: English only; dates month-level; no storytelling; no marketing adjectives; every statement is verifiable.

<<<P4-S9>>>
4.9 README STANDARD
Purpose: the front door. It says what the project is and is not, how it is built and the essentials. Depth lives in docs/ and is linked. No storytelling, no code samples, no marketing adjectives.
Order of content
1. Lines 1 and 2: the honesty statement (proposed text: "A person's worth lies in their honesty." and "This holds regardless of position, title or role.").
2. Hero capture: the animated image of 4.10.
3. Project name, one-line tagline, truthful badges (build status, version 1, license, level label).
4. Level label "Enterprise Production-Grade Tier 5" with a short explanation that it is the target level the project is being built toward, not a present claim.
5. Author block: "AI Architect Amin Azimi — End to End System Development & Business Challenge"; "Azimi Innovation Lab"; project name; version 1; month.
6. What it is, is not, does, does not (compact table).
7. Problem and outcome (at most five sentences).
8. Architecture at a glance: one diagram (pipeline and six roles); link to docs.
9. Capabilities index: 40 rows (id, name, one-line purpose, status mark); link to docs.
10. Trust model: label legend, evidence-or-abstain, claims-versus-reality table, and a table of known limits and planned work generated from `docs/planned-work.toml` (each row names the register id it maps to; the table sits inside this section and adds no section).
11. Build Progress: a table of every phase; a completed phase shows a green tick and its month, set by the gate script in the phase-end task (Part 1 §10.3); pending phases show an empty box.
12. Quality gates summary and link to test-house/.
13. Requirements and standards conformance: a table mapping the project's stated requirements and the engineering standards it follows to the tests that demonstrate them. It makes no comparison with other projects or events.
14. Data, security and license summary with links; the license is proprietary, all rights reserved.
15. Usage guide link, version, month.
Visual style: the owner's chosen fusion (mission control with blueprint) applied with restraint: one accent color, aligned tables, consistent spacing, no decorative emoji; the tick mark is the only symbol used for progress. Dates are month-level.
README checks: a gate verifies the order of sections, the presence of the required lines, absence of code blocks, absence of forbidden patterns, and that every table row maps to repository facts.

<<<P4-S10>>>
4.10 HERO CAPTURE (README ANIMATION)
- Content: about 30 seconds of the running OpenAPI interactive documentation, showing a complete synthetic case from creation to Passport. All data is SYNTHETIC and the capture carries an on-screen label saying so.
- Production: a scripted browser capture (no manual recording) against a locally started stack with seeded data and fixed timing, assembled by the project-owned encoder from screenshot frames into an optimized animated PNG of at most 960 pixels width, at most 12 frames per second, at most 5 MB, looping, with descriptive alternative text and a static poster frame; the frame sequence and its timing file are stored under docs/media.
- Reproducibility: the capture script and its seed live under tools/media; a gate checks size, duration, dimensions and that no personal data or real hostnames appear.
- Timing: produced in the release-preparation phase, after the API and views are final.

<<<P4-S11>>>
4.11 COMMITS, PULL REQUESTS AND RELEASES
- Commits and merges follow Part 1 (§4, §10). Pull request body fixed fields: phase, tasks with commit hashes, generated gate table (gate, command, exit code), self-review reference, updated artifacts. No signature or attribution line of any tool, model or agent.
- Versioning: semantic versioning; the release candidate is 1.0.0; a changelog in Keep a Changelog form is updated in the same task as the change it describes.
- Release: tag, signed artifacts where supported, SBOM attached, restore drill passed, final audits passed, directive replaced by its two-line stub (Part 7).

<<<P4-S12>>>
4.12 LICENSE, DEPENDENCY POLICY AND HYGIENE
- Project license: proprietary, all rights reserved; the LICENSE file states exactly that and nothing else about origin or purpose.
- Dependency licenses must be on the allowlist: MIT, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, Apache-2.0, ISC, PSF-2.0, MPL-2.0 (unmodified use only). MIT-0, 0BSD, Zlib and CC0-1.0 are admitted only for the two named transitive packages of decision D-37 (cffi, numpy) and for no other package. Any other license, including GPL, LGPL and AGPL families, fails the license gate. Rationale: the owner must stay free to sell, gift or relicense the whole work.
- Every dependency is recorded with name, version, license identifier (SPDX) and purpose; unused dependencies fail the gate.
- The allowlist controls inputs only. The licence of the work itself is proprietary, all rights reserved, and no dependency licence on the allowlist restricts selling, assigning, gifting or sublicensing the work or any of its modules.
- The check covers every package in the lock, direct and transitive. A package whose licence expression has any component outside the allowlist fails the gate. A new version of any package triggers a new licence check before the lock changes.
- `docs/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` lists every package and vendored asset with name, version, licence identifier and the licence text or attribution the licence requires. It is generated by the licence gate from the lock and the vendored assets, never edited by hand; a stale file fails the gate. Before P31 the file is generated into the working tree but ignored by git and not committed; task P31-T05 commits it and removes the ignore entry.
- Third-party copyrighted tables and texts are not stored (Part 3 §3.12).
- Hygiene gate: forbidden patterns are held outside the repository (CI secret store) in two lists. `HYGIENE_PATTERNS` (always forbidden) holds the names of development agents and editors and process words, and matches in every tracked file, commit message and pull request text without exception. `HYGIENE_PATTERNS_VENDOR` (forbidden except in declared paths) holds model-provider names, which the product legitimately needs in its provider adapters; it is skipped only for files whose path matches an entry of the protected file `tools/gate/hygiene_scope.toml` (entry: path glob, reason, owner decision id; allowed only under the adapter, provider-configuration, provider-test and provider-documentation paths), and never for commit messages, pull request text, the README or this directive. The gate fails closed: a missing or empty list, or a list under the minimum entry count, is a failure, not a pass. The gate reports only pass or fail and the file path. The project is agent-neutral: the patterns include the names of development agents, editors and their vendors, so no repository file, commit message or pull request text names the agent that produced it, and any capable agent can continue the work from the repository state alone. Authority and local behaviour: CI is the only place where the lists exist, so the full scan runs there and its pass is a required check of the pull request. Where the lists are not present (every local run by the agent) the gate does not fail and does not stop the run: it reports the status NOT_RUN_LOCAL, which is listed in the gate table as pending in CI, is never counted as passed and never read as a pass by `gate phase`. The agent is never given the lists, a path to them or an environment that holds them; if it finds them, that is a STOP (Part 1 §7.1). The structural checks that need no list (below, `gate commit-check`) run locally and in CI.

<<<P4-S13>>>
4.13 USER INTERFACE STANDARD
- Accessibility with WCAG 2.2 level AA as the target: keyboard operation, visible focus, contrast, semantic markup, labelled controls; automated checks in CI. Automated checks cover only part of WCAG, so a clean automated result is reported as "zero automated violations" and never as conformance; the word AA (or "WCAG conformant") may be used as a statement about the product only with a reference to a recorded manual audit (`docs/decisions/accessibility-audit.md`, owner-recorded, status NOT_AUDITED until the owner records an audit).
- Bilingual readiness: Arabic and English text, right-to-left layout support, Unicode-safe rendering and number display.
- Server-rendered pages with minimal progressive enhancement; every state change goes through the API contract; destructive or approving actions require explicit confirmation and show the exact proposal hash.
- Clear states for empty, loading, error and blocked conditions; every error shows its stable code.

<<<P4-S14>>>
4.14 PERFORMANCE AND RELIABILITY BUDGETS (design parameters; measured on the reference environment recorded in docs)
- Deterministic evaluation of a case with 200 facts: p95 at most 200 ms.
- API read operations: p95 at most 300 ms; mutating operations excluding model calls: p95 at most 500 ms.
- Ledger verification: 10,000 records in at most 5 seconds.
- Replay of a case: at most 2 seconds without model calls.
- Startup to readiness: at most 30 seconds.
- Budgets are tests (perf gate); a regression beyond 10 percent of the ratchet baseline fails the gate.

<<<P4-S15>>>
4.15 TOKEN ECONOMY OF THE BUILD
Running a check costs computing time, not agent tokens; tokens are spent only on what the agent reads or writes. The standards therefore move all verification into scripts and keep what the agent reads small.
- Scripts verify, the agent reads results: the agent reads an exit code and at most 20 lines of a result (Part 1 §3.8); the gate table in reports and pull requests is generated.
- State over history: the agent reads the state file and the previous phase report only, never earlier phases (Part 1 §2, Part 3 §3.29).
- Read by marker: the agent reads only the Part 1 block, the phase block and its Context Pack (Part 1 §2.1).
- Generate, do not write: code, schemas, validators, fixtures, test skeletons, references and indices come from generators at build time; the agent edits definitions, not generated output.
- No duplication: one source of truth per fact; reports reference ids, not restated text.
- Failures cost tokens, so diagnosis is bounded: three attempts with a written one-line diagnosis each (Part 1 §7.3).
- Phase entry and exit checks are script-driven comparisons of state, ledger and history; they do not re-read previous work.
<<<END-PART-4>>>

<<<PART-5>>>
PART 5 — CAPABILITY SPECIFICATIONS

<<<P5-S1>>>
5.1 CONVENTIONS (apply to every capability)

5.1.1 Spec template. Each capability section contains, in this order:
H header: ID, name, version, class, lead role, depends, output kind, product requirements covered.
T1 Purpose and user job. T2 Inputs (typed facts: key, type, unit, source document type, required or optional). T3 Sources and labels (source ids; gaps listed as UNVERIFIED). T4 Logic (rules, formulas, solver model, comparators, validity windows). T5 Outputs (typed; contribution to the Decision). T6 Reason codes (closed table: code, meaning, outcome). T7 Abstention, hold and escalation conditions. T8 Evidence records. T9 Budget use. T10 Interfaces (API, CLI, view). T11 Scenarios (table with expected outcome). T12 Tests (property, metamorphic, differential, adversarial, replay). T13 Responsible-use notes. T14 Consumers (look-ahead).
Probabilistic capabilities (class P) add T4a: nonconformity score definition, calibration set specification (SYNTHETIC), miscoverage level, set-size rule (Part 3 §3.13), and the exact wording of the coverage claim.

5.1.2 Identifiers. Reason codes: `<CAP>.<GROUP>.<NAME>`, uppercase, closed per capability, never reused. Scenarios: `<CAP>-S<nn>`. Findings: identifier derived by hashing capability id, kind, sorted fact ids and pack version, so the same inputs give the same identifiers.

5.1.3 Uniform interface. API: `POST /v1/cases/{case_id}/capabilities/{cap_id}/evaluations` (idempotency key required) and `GET /v1/cases/{case_id}/capabilities/{cap_id}/evaluations/{evaluation_id}`. Command line: the evaluate command with a capability filter. View: a tab on the case page with a stable anchor per capability. Errors are RFC 9457 problem details with stable codes.
Tenant-scope route: a capability that works across the cases of one tenant (period reports, roll-ups, re-screening runs) is invoked with `POST /v1/capabilities/{cap_id}/evaluations` and read with `GET /v1/capabilities/{cap_id}/evaluations/{evaluation_id}`; the tenant is taken from the credential only, and a result never contains another tenant's data. The command line and the view follow the same split.

5.1.4 Standard outcome mapping (a capability may be stricter, never looser):
- A rule that reads a CONFLICTED fact: ESCALATE with both citations.
- A required fact missing, UNKNOWN or only PROPOSED: ABSTAIN.
- A concrete defect that blocks progress until corrected: HOLD.
- A matter that needs human judgement: ESCALATE.
- ALLOW only when all required facts are VERIFIED, no rule fails, and the label condition of Part 3 §3.8 holds (read with decision D-26).
Capabilities whose output kind is ADV or REP never return ALLOW, HOLD or ESCALATE. Their status is PROVIDED (a result was produced; a qualified result carries an INDICATIVE or PARTIAL reason code), NEEDS_REVIEW (a person must look before the result is used), ABSTAINED (a required fact is missing or not VERIFIED) or REFUSED (the request is outside the capability's boundary); a CONFLICTED fact gives NEEDS_REVIEW with both citations.
Any internal error, invalid pack or budget breach yields ABSTAIN or ESCALATE, never ALLOW (I5).

5.1.5 Definition of Done (12 items; all must hold, each shown by named tests or gate output):
1. Spec sections H and T1–T14 complete, with no placeholders.
2. Pack manifest, fact schema and sources registered with labels and licenses; no unlabelled datum.
3. Logic implemented deterministically with explicit evaluation time; validity windows tested at both edges.
4. Reason codes closed; each code reachable by at least one named test.
5. Every abstention, hold and escalation condition in T7 has a test.
6. Scenarios meet the floor (Part 2 §2.13) and run as golden tests, including negative and adversarial cases.
7. Property and metamorphic tests for the stated invariants; a differential test where two implementations exist.
8. Evidence records emitted; replay from the ledger reproduces the identical output hash.
9. Item 9 has two parts. 9a: API route, command, view stub (standard layout) and generated OpenAPI present and registered. 9b: the accessibility check passes on the view; it is run by the `a11y` gate, which is active from P27 (Part 6 §6.13), so before P27 item 9b is recorded as PENDING-P27 and is not counted as open.
10. Budget and performance within Part 3 §3.14 and Part 4 §4.14; no model call outside the declared stage.
11. Adversarial corpus passes (instruction injection, confusable characters, oversized and malformed input); tenant-isolation test covers the capability's tables and caches.
12. Coverage and mutation thresholds (Part 4 §4.5) met for the capability's modules; traceability row linking PR items, scenarios and tests is generated; label and source audit passes.

5.1.6 Configuration, not data. Comparator settings, tolerances and bands are configuration recorded under the configuration hash and shown with every output; they are not labelled data (see D-26). They are not free of provenance either: calibrated and statistical threshold artifacts carry a validation class under §5.1.6a.

5.1.6a Calibration basis (decision D-43). Every capability whose ALLOW rests on a calibrated or statistical threshold artifact (a conformal quantile, a matching band, an interval, a deviation threshold) lists each such artifact in its output field `calibration_basis`: artifact id, hash and validation class. The classes are CALIBRATED_SYNTHETIC (made only from SYNTHETIC data; the default) and VALIDATED_TENANT (the artifact passed a tenant validation, below). For a case whose `data_origin` is not SYNTHETIC, a capability that would give ALLOW while any artifact of its calibration basis is CALIBRATED_SYNTHETIC gives ESCALATE with the reason `<ID>.REVIEW.UNVALIDATED_CALIBRATION`; for a SYNTHETIC case the ALLOW stands and is stamped (D-26). The ALLOW eligibility function of the kernel enforces this, so no capability can bypass it. Capabilities concerned: F02 (quantile), K01 (bands), K05 (interval), K21 (thresholds); a capability with such an artifact that does not declare it fails the conformance test of the generator.
Tenant validation. A validation run evaluates the artifact on at least 300 pairs labelled by the tenant's own reviewers (precedent memory M3, reviewer-confirmed outcomes) or delivered through the feed OUTCOME_CONFIRMATIONS of K16 (envelope E0.4; the statistic label TENANT_OUTCOMES, a statistic label and never a data label); 300 is a design value (ASSUMPTION) that the owner may change in `docs/decisions/`. The pass criterion is stated in the configuration of each capability (coverage for F02 and K05, recall at the band for K01, an error bound for K21); the default for coverage is a Wilson 95 percent lower bound of at least 1 − α, and F02 adds that the Wilson upper bound of the error rate among lines with a one-member set is at most the target in the artifact configuration (default α), because marginal coverage says nothing about that subset. A capability without a stated pass criterion cannot reach VALIDATED_TENANT. The result, the size, the hash of the labelled set (never its content) and the artifact hash are recorded on the tenant system chain. The class becomes VALIDATED_TENANT only through an activation made like a pack activation, with a second reviewer; it lapses when the pack version, the artifact or the labelled set changes and after the revalidation period in the tenant configuration; it also lapses when monitoring of the artifact has been NOT_MEASURABLE for longer than that period (Part 3 §3.13). A validation never changes the label of any datum.

5.1.7 Reading discipline. Each capability section is bracketed by `<<<P5-xxx>>>` markers so an agent reads only the section its phase lists.

5.1.8 Staleness. An evaluation is bound to the hash of the fact set, pack versions and configuration it read. When any bound input changes, the evaluation is marked STALE; it stays in the ledger unchanged, is never used for a Decision, and the workflow recomputes it, so the new evaluation references the stale one. Recomputation after a pack or list change runs in batches of up to 200 cases per run.

5.1.9 Workflow completeness. The outcome of a case is final only when every capability the workflow configures for it has a non-stale evaluation; otherwise the outcome is ABSTAIN with reason WORKFLOW.INCOMPLETE. A capability that reports NOT_APPLICABLE counts as complete.

5.1.10 Matching modes. A capability that compares identities or texts names its mode in T4. PROTECTIVE folds look-alike characters and script variants before comparing, so a swapped or substituted character cannot hide a match; it may produce extra candidates, which a human resolves. NORMALIZED applies Unicode normalization and digit and script normalization only. EXACT compares the normalized form byte for byte. A capability may be stricter than its stated mode, never looser. These three are the only matching modes: a capability text never names any other mode (the word STRICT is not a mode), and the linter checks every `Mode:` line against the list (LINT-23). Where a capability needs to treat a look-alike character as a difference, the mode is NORMALIZED for names and EXACT for identifiers, numbers and seals.

<<<P5-F01>>>
5.F01 CONSISTENCY GRAPH
H: F01 · Consistency Graph · v1.0.0 · class X+D · lead: Intake and Extraction · depends: kernel only · output: DEC · covers PR-01, PR-02, PR-03, PR-11, PR-16, PR-17.

T1 Purpose and user job. A shipment is described by several documents that must tell one story. F01 compares the verified facts of all documents in a case, reports every contradiction with exact citations, checks the arithmetic inside and across documents, and publishes the agreed shipment values that other capabilities consume. Job: let a broker see, before filing, where documents disagree and why.

T2 Inputs. Only facts with status VERIFIED are read. Facts are document-scoped (key form `<document_type>.<field>`) and arrive from the Extract stage with locator and method. Generic document types: COMMERCIAL_INVOICE, PACKING_LIST, TRANSPORT_DOCUMENT, ORIGIN_CERTIFICATE, DELIVERY_ORDER, INSURANCE_CERTIFICATE, DECLARATION_DRAFT. Fields compared (each optional per document type, required per workflow configuration): seller_name, buyer_name, consignee_name, goods_description, line items (description, quantity, unit, unit_price, line_amount), total_quantity, package_count, package_gross_weight (per package), gross_weight, net_weight, invoice_total, currency, incoterm, port_of_loading, port_of_discharge, container_numbers, transport_document_number, invoice_number, declared_classification_code, shipped_on_board_date. The required document set is workflow configuration, not hard-coded.

T3 Sources and labels. F01 uses no external reference data except (a) a legal-form suffix table for party-name normalization, a pack list with label DERIVED from the pack author's compilation, and (b) the container identification check-digit procedure, named by standard identifier and edition only (its text is not stored) and recorded in the Source Registry with the label DERIVED from the owner's reading of the official description once V-10 part a is closed, a precondition of P14; should the registry status ever be UNVERIFIED (for example after a registry downgrade), the check-digit rule is disabled and its absence is reported as F01.ABSTAIN.RULE_UNAVAILABLE rather than assumed to pass. Case facts carry the labels assigned by the Kernel (read with D-26).

T4 Logic. All comparisons use the facts' normalized values; money, quantities and weights are fixed-point decimals with units (Part 3 §3.3).
a) Normalization (shared Kernel functions, applied before comparison): Unicode NFKC, case folding, whitespace collapse, zero-width and bidirectional control characters removed, Arabic-Indic and Persian digits mapped to ASCII with both forms retained. Characters that merely look alike (confusables) are not merged; a Latin and a Cyrillic letter differ.
b) Agreement sets. An agreement set lists the document-scoped keys that must carry one value and a comparator. Comparators:
 - NAME: normalized equality after removing the registered legal-form suffixes.
 - EXACT: normalized string equality (identifiers, ports, incoterm, currency, document numbers).
 - SET: equality of normalized sets (container numbers).
 - QUANTITY: unit-normalized exact decimals; every stated value v with d decimal places is the closed interval [v - u/2, v + u/2] where u is one unit of its last stated digit (u = 10 to the power minus d); a count that the schema declares exact has width zero; unit conversion scales the interval by the exact factor. Any number of values agree when the intersection of all their intervals is non-empty, which is independent of order and of pairing; the finding reports the common interval. Configuration may widen every interval by an absolute tolerance, default zero. A value whose number of decimals cannot be read from the source is not VERIFIED for this comparator.
 - MONEY: same currency required; amounts equal at the currency's minor-unit precision.
 - PREFIX: for classification codes, equality over the length of the shorter code.
 - TOKENS: normalized token-set similarity (Jaccard) with two configured bands; above the upper band agree; between the bands informational only; below the lower band mismatch.
 - DATE: equality of the same event date.
c) Arithmetic constraints, each evaluated in exact decimal arithmetic:
 - ARI-1 sum of line amounts equals invoice total (MONEY precision).
 - ARI-2 quantity times unit price equals line amount.
 - ARI-3 the interval sum of the package gross weights (lower bounds added and upper bounds added) intersects the interval of the gross weight (QUANTITY rule).
 - ARI-4 net weight does not exceed gross weight: violated only when the lower bound of the net weight exceeds the upper bound of the gross weight.
 - ARI-5 the interval sum of the line quantities per unit intersects the interval of the total quantity (QUANTITY rule).
 - ARI-6 number of packages listed equals package_count.
 A constraint is evaluated only when every operand is VERIFIED; otherwise it is reported as not evaluable.
d) Z3 role. The decimal evaluator is the primary implementation. The same constraints are encoded for Z3 as a second implementation used (i) in the differential test and (ii) at run time to extract a minimal unsatisfiable core, which becomes the minimal set of cited facts in the finding. A disagreement between the two implementations is a defect and yields ESCALATE with reason F01.ESCALATE.SOLVER_DISAGREEMENT.
e) Identifier validity: container numbers must satisfy the registered check-digit procedure (see T3).
f) Canonical values. For each agreement set that fully agrees, F01 requests the Kernel write path to record one canonical shipment fact (key `shipment.<field>`), status VERIFIED, label DERIVED, source = the agreeing fact ids. Where a set does not agree, no canonical fact is produced and downstream capabilities see ABSTAIN.
g) Combination. Outcome = most severe finding (ALLOW < ABSTAIN < HOLD < ESCALATE); all reasons are kept.

T5 Outputs. ConsistencyReport: case id, evaluation time, configuration hash, pack versions, graph (nodes = fact ids; edges = AGREES, CONTRADICTS, ARITHMETIC with set or constraint id), findings list, agreement sets checked and not evaluable, counts by kind, outcome and reason codes. Finding: deterministic id, kind, reason code, cited facts (document id, locator, normalized value, original text), unsatisfiable core when arithmetic, and a template-rendered explanation (PR-17). Canonical fact requests as in T4f. Contribution to the Decision: outcome and reason codes.

T6 Reason codes.
| Code | Meaning | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| F01.OK.ALL_AGREE | all evaluated sets agree, all constraints hold, nothing required is missing | ALLOW (subject to D-26 label condition) |
| F01.MISMATCH.PARTY | seller, buyer or consignee differ across documents | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.IDENTIFIER | document number, container set or other identifier differs | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.QUANTITY | quantities or package counts differ | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.WEIGHT | gross or net weights differ | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.VALUE | invoice totals or amounts differ across documents | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.CURRENCY | currencies differ | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.ROUTE | ports of loading or discharge differ | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.TERMS | incoterm differs | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.CLASSIFICATION | declared classification codes differ | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.DESCRIPTION | goods description similarity below the lower band | ESCALATE |
| F01.MISMATCH.DATE | the same event dated differently | ESCALATE |
| F01.FACT.CONFLICTED | one document yields two non-superseded values for a field | ESCALATE |
| F01.ARITH.SUM_VALUE | line amounts do not sum to invoice total | HOLD |
| F01.ARITH.LINE_AMOUNT | quantity times unit price differs from line amount | HOLD |
| F01.ARITH.SUM_WEIGHT | package weights do not sum to gross weight | HOLD |
| F01.ARITH.NET_GT_GROSS | net weight exceeds gross weight | HOLD |
| F01.ARITH.SUM_QUANTITY | line quantities do not sum to total quantity | HOLD |
| F01.ARITH.PACKAGES | listed packages differ from package count | HOLD |
| F01.ID.CHECK_DIGIT | a container number fails the check-digit procedure | HOLD |
| F01.ABSTAIN.DOC_NOT_PRESENT | a required document type is absent | ABSTAIN |
| F01.ABSTAIN.MISSING_COUNTERPART | a key exists in only one document of a set that needs two | ABSTAIN |
| F01.ABSTAIN.FACT_NOT_VERIFIED | a needed fact is PROPOSED or UNKNOWN | ABSTAIN |
| F01.ABSTAIN.LABEL_INSUFFICIENT | a contributing datum is weaker than the ALLOW condition | ABSTAIN |
| F01.ABSTAIN.RULE_UNAVAILABLE | a rule is disabled (for example the unverified check-digit source) | ABSTAIN |
| F01.ESCALATE.SOLVER_DISAGREEMENT | decimal evaluator and Z3 disagree | ESCALATE |

T7 Conditions. Abstain: any required document type absent; any required key without two comparable verified values; any needed fact not VERIFIED; any disabled rule; label condition unmet. Hold: any arithmetic violation or invalid identifier. Escalate: any mismatch, any conflicted fact, any solver disagreement. The evaluation never edits a document or a fact and never proposes a corrected value.

T8 Evidence. One evaluation record per run holding: commitment to the input fact ids and values, configuration hash, pack and capability versions, evaluation time, output hash; and one record per finding with its citations. Canonical-fact requests are separate Kernel records. Replay recomputes the report from the recorded inputs and must match the output hash.

T9 Budget. No model call (the model is used only in the Extract stage, accounted there). Pure computation; within the evaluation budget of Part 4 §4.14 for a case of up to 6 documents and 500 facts.

T10 Interfaces. API and command line per 5.1.3. View: a Consistency tab on the case page showing documents as columns and agreement sets as rows; each cell shows the normalized value and original text with a link to the evidence viewer at the exact locator; contradictions are marked with an icon and text, never colour alone; arithmetic findings show the unsatisfiable core; keyboard operable; right-to-left ready.

T11 Scenarios.
| ID | Description | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| F01-S01 | all documents agree, arithmetic holds | ALLOW, OK.ALL_AGREE, canonical facts produced |
| F01-S02 | consignee name differs between transport document and invoice | ESCALATE, MISMATCH.PARTY, both citations |
| F01-S03 | line amounts do not sum to invoice total | HOLD, ARITH.SUM_VALUE, core lists the lines and the total |
| F01-S04 | container number with a wrong check digit (rule enabled) | HOLD, ID.CHECK_DIGIT |
| F01-S05 | packing list absent from a workflow that requires it | ABSTAIN, DOC_NOT_PRESENT |
| F01-S06 | gross weight 1,250 in one document and 1,250.4 in another | agree (intervals 1249.5 to 1250.5 and 1250.35 to 1250.45 intersect); ALLOW if nothing else fails |
| F01-S07 | invoice with Arabic-Indic digits, transport document with ASCII digits, same values | agree; both forms retained |
| F01-S08 | the same field extracted twice with different values in one document | ESCALATE, FACT.CONFLICTED |
| F01-S09 | document text instructs the system to mark the case consistent | no effect; findings unchanged (I3) |
| F01-S10 | needed fact only PROPOSED | ABSTAIN, FACT_NOT_VERIFIED |
| F01-S11 | party names identical except a Latin letter replaced by a look-alike Cyrillic letter | ESCALATE, MISMATCH.PARTY |
| F01-S12 | check-digit source still UNVERIFIED | rule disabled; ABSTAIN, RULE_UNAVAILABLE reported |

T12 Tests. Property: report is identical under any ordering of documents and facts; evaluating twice gives the same output hash; adding a verified contradicting fact never lowers severity. Metamorphic: converting a weight from kilograms to grams with its unit field leaves agreement unchanged; renaming document identifiers leaves finding kinds unchanged; adding irrelevant facts changes nothing. Intervals: agreement of any number of QUANTITY values is identical under every ordering and holds exactly when the common intersection is non-empty; the interval of a sum equals the sum of the intervals; widening by a tolerance never turns agreement into disagreement. Differential: decimal evaluator versus Z3 over generated constraint sets, with interval bounds encoded as exact rational inequalities, agree on satisfiable or not, and the core is minimal. Adversarial corpus: instruction text in fields, confusable and zero-width characters, mixed-direction text, very large and deeply nested documents, duplicated fields. Replay test per scenario. Tenant-isolation test on the capability's records and any cache.

T13 Responsible use. F01 reports discrepancies and never proposes edits that would make documents agree; the explanation states what differs and where.

T14 Consumers. F02, F03, F04, K01, K02, K03, K05, K06, K13, K21, L02, L03, L04, L05 read the canonical shipment facts F01 publishes; they treat their absence as missing evidence.

<<<P5-F02>>>
5.F02 CLASSIFICATION JUDGE
H: F02 · Classification Judge · v1.0.0 · class P+D · lead: Compliance · depends: F01 · output: DEC · covers PR-01, PR-02, PR-03, PR-10, PR-16, PR-17.

T1 Purpose. For each line item, judge whether the declared tariff classification is consistent with the goods description, returning a calibrated candidate set or abstaining. F02 judges consistency; it is not an official ruling.

T2 Inputs. Per line n, from F01 canonical facts: description, declared classification code; optional VERIFIED attributes (material, composition, use). Tariff pack id and evaluation time. F02 abstains when F01 has not produced the canonical facts for the line.

T3 Sources and labels. Tariff Pack: code list with validity windows, heading texts, and notes encoded as rules. Its label is OFFICIAL only when loaded from an official source whose licence permits this use; otherwise SECONDARY or UNVERIFIED, which blocks ALLOW (V-04, V-09). Tests use a SYNTHETIC mini-tariff. Encoded notes are labelled DERIVED. Code levels (digits per level) are declared by the pack, not assumed.

T4 Logic.
a) Validity. The declared code must exist in the pack at the pack's level and be valid at the evaluation time; otherwise HOLD.
b) Scorer (deterministic, model-free). Description and attributes are normalized (F01 T4a), pack stopwords removed, and digit-only tokens discarded so a code typed into a description has no effect. Similarity is a saturating term-weight overlap (BM25 form, parameters fixed in the pack) between the description and the concatenated text path of each candidate (chapter, heading, subheading), divided by the candidate's self-score to give sim in [0, 1]. Nonconformity s(x, y) = 1 − sim(x, y).
b2) Languages. The pack manifest lists the languages the pack covers; the default is English only. A pack may carry a closed bilingual glossary (label DERIVED from the pack author's compilation, never a model translation) that maps Arabic description tokens to the pack's English vocabulary before scoring; the expansion is deterministic and recorded per line. A description whose Arabic tokens are not covered by the glossary beyond a threshold fixed in the pack is ABSTAIN with F02.ABSTAIN.LANGUAGE_UNSUPPORTED. Calibration is per language: a language is served only if a calibration set of that language is bound to the pack version and meets the size rules of c); otherwise LANGUAGE_UNSUPPORTED. Without a glossary the capability is English only, and the README and every Passport state the languages covered by the active pack.
c) Calibration (split conformal, Part 3 §3.13). q̂ is the ⌈(n+1)(1−α)⌉-th smallest nonconformity of the true codes in the calibration set of n pairs, α = 0.10. The calibration set is stored with its hash, bound to the tariff pack version hash, and generated by the Synthetic Data Factory in two layers: (1) variants of the pack's own texts (paraphrase, abbreviation, ordering, noise); (2) commercial-name variants drawn from a separate lexicon of trade names, synonyms and misspellings that is not derived from the tariff texts, so true-code scores are not optimistic by construction. n ≥ max(500, 10 × C), where C is the number of chapters present in the tariff pack (read from the pack and recorded in the artifact, never a fixed number), and every chapter is present at least 10 times, else F02 abstains with CALIBRATION_STALE. The floor of 10 per chapter is the least safe value: the class-conditional quantile ⌈(n_c+1)(1−α)⌉-th smallest exists only when ⌈(n_c+1)(1−α)⌉ ≤ n_c, which holds from n_c = 9 at α = 0.10 and fails at 8. A shifted validation set (heavier noise, longer descriptions, different seed) is generated separately and used only for the diagnostic in T4a.
d) Candidate set C = {y valid at evaluation time : s(x, y) ≤ q̂}, ordered by score then code.
e) Rule filters. Encoded tariff notes may remove candidates only when their applicability facts are VERIFIED; each removal is recorded. The coverage claim refers to C before filtering.
f) Resolution level. If the declared code is deeper than the level the scorer resolves, comparison uses the shorter prefix and the output states the resolved level.
g) Decision per line (the case outcome is the most severe line):
 - set empty or larger than 3: ABSTAIN;
 - declared code in C and C has one member: ALLOW when the label condition per D-26 and the calibration basis of Part 5 §5.1.6a hold, otherwise ESCALATE with F02.REVIEW.UNVALIDATED_CALIBRATION;
 - declared code in C and C has 2 or 3 members: ESCALATE (ambiguity);
 - declared code outside C: ESCALATE, with the candidates listed.

T4a Probabilistic basis. Score as in b); calibration as in c); set-size rule 3. Coverage claim, verbatim: "On the SYNTHETIC calibration distribution, and assuming exchangeability, the true code lies in the candidate set with probability at least 90 percent (marginal). This statement does not apply to real goods descriptions or to any single chapter." Diagnostics generated with every calibration: per-chapter empirical coverage on a held-out split, and coverage on the shifted validation set (expected to be lower; reported, never hidden). Neither diagnostic is a guarantee. The coverage claim is made per language and names the language of the calibration set it rests on.

T5 Outputs. Per line: declared code, resolved level, candidate set (code, score, rank), q̂, α, calibration set id and hash, exclusions applied, outcome, reason codes, the coverage claim with its basis, and a template-rendered explanation. Contribution to the Decision: outcome.

T6 Reason codes.
| Code | Meaning | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| F02.OK.CONSISTENT | declared code is the only candidate | ALLOW |
| F02.AMBIGUOUS.MULTIPLE_CANDIDATES | declared code is in a set of 2 or 3 | ESCALATE |
| F02.MISMATCH.OUTSIDE_SET | declared code not in the set | ESCALATE |
| F02.CODE.INVALID | declared code not in the tariff pack | HOLD |
| F02.CODE.NOT_VALID_AT_TIME | declared code exists but is not valid at the evaluation time | HOLD |
| F02.ABSTAIN.SET_EMPTY | no candidate within q̂ | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.SET_TOO_LARGE | more than 3 candidates | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.DESCRIPTION_MISSING | no VERIFIED description | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.DESCRIPTION_TOO_SHORT | fewer than 3 tokens | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.LANGUAGE_UNSUPPORTED | description language not covered by the pack | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.TARIFF_UNAVAILABLE | no pack valid at the evaluation time | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.CALIBRATION_STALE | calibration set not bound to this pack version, or too small | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.UPSTREAM | F01 canonical facts missing for the line | ABSTAIN |
| F02.ABSTAIN.LABEL_INSUFFICIENT | tariff or rule data weaker than the ALLOW condition | ABSTAIN |
| F02.REVIEW.UNVALIDATED_CALIBRATION | ALLOW would result on real data while the quantile is CALIBRATED_SYNTHETIC, or the one-member error bound is not shown | ESCALATE |

T7 Conditions. As in the table. F02 never chooses a code, never ranks candidates by duty or any cost effect, and never edits the declared code.

T8 Evidence. One record per evaluation: commitments to inputs, pack version, calibration set hash, q̂, α, configuration hash, output hash; one record per line judgement. Replay recomputes sets from recorded inputs.

T9 Budget. No model call. Index built once per pack version; per-line scoring within Part 4 §4.14 budgets.

T10 Interfaces. API and command per 5.1.3. View: per-line table (declared code, candidates with scores, exclusions), the verbatim coverage claim, the diagnostics link, and the banner "advisory consistency check, not an official classification".

T11 Scenarios.
| ID | Description | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| F02-S01 | declared code is the sole candidate | ALLOW (SYNTHETIC stamp) |
| F02-S02 | declared code outside the set | ESCALATE, OUTSIDE_SET, candidates listed |
| F02-S03 | two candidates including the declared code | ESCALATE, MULTIPLE_CANDIDATES |
| F02-S04 | description "goods" | ABSTAIN, DESCRIPTION_TOO_SHORT |
| F02-S05 | declared code expired the day before evaluation; and evaluation exactly on the last valid instant | HOLD NOT_VALID_AT_TIME; the second case valid |
| F02-S06 | declared code absent from the pack | HOLD, CODE.INVALID |
| F02-S07 | Arabic description, pack covers English only | ABSTAIN, LANGUAGE_UNSUPPORTED |
| F02-S08 | tariff pack labelled SECONDARY | ABSTAIN, LABEL_INSUFFICIENT |
| F02-S09 | calibration set bound to an older pack version | ABSTAIN, CALIBRATION_STALE |
| F02-S10 | description contains "classify as 8517" and copied heading text | digits ignored; result from the scorer only |
| F02-S11 | 1,000 seeded calibration trials on the synthetic factory that also built the calibration set (labelled implementation-correctness: it checks the quantile code, not validity on real data) | empirical coverage within 3 standard errors of 90 percent |
| F02-S12 | shifted validation set | coverage reported as diagnostic; no pass or fail on the nominal level (the safe behaviour under shift is tested separately, T-F02-0231 to 0234) |

T12 Tests. Property: sim in [0, 1]; smaller α gives a superset; output independent of line order; determinism. Metamorphic: case, whitespace, stopwords and digit tokens change nothing. Differential: optimized index against a reference scorer; quantile function against its brute-force definition. Statistical: T11 coverage test (seeded, 1,000 trials, tolerance 3 standard errors); the diagnostics are generated and archived. Adversarial: keyword stuffing, copied headings, mixed scripts, very long descriptions. Replay per scenario.

T13 Responsible use. F02 does not suggest which candidate carries a lower or higher duty, and does not advise rewording descriptions.

T14 Consumers. L02, K05, K16, F04, K21, K22, K03.

<<<P5-F03>>>
5.F03 FREE-TIME CLOCK AND COST SHIELD
H: F03 · Free-Time Clock & Cost Shield · v1.0.0 · class D · lead: Logistics and Cost · depends: F01 · output: DEC · covers PR-01, PR-02, PR-03, PR-13, PR-15, PR-16.

T1 Purpose. For every container, compute how much free time remains, what is accruing, and which timely actions avoid charges, so action happens before money is lost.

T2 Inputs. Per container: container number (F01 canonical set), type and size; movement events with instants and the source of their time zone (discharged, gated out full, gated in empty); tariff terms per charge kind (demurrage, detention, storage): free days, day basis (CALENDAR or WORKING), start event, stop event, day-one rule, cut-off time and time zone, partial-day rule, rate tiers per container type, rounding mode, calendar id; clock suspension windows stated by the terms or recorded as VERIFIED facts with their source (periods in which the tariff does not count days, such as recorded terminal closures or authority holds); evaluation time (explicit parameter, recorded).

T3 Sources and labels. Tariff terms come from a registered published tariff (OFFICIAL if the source permits) or from the tenant's contract document (a case fact, DERIVED). Calendars come from versioned calendar packs; weekend and holiday data stays UNVERIFIED until V-06 is closed, which blocks ALLOW whenever the WORKING basis depends on it. A suspension window counts only when VERIFIED.

T4 Logic.
a) Clock. For each charge kind: start instant from the start event, stop instant from the stop event (absent while running). Day boundaries follow the tariff time zone.
