| L06-S01 | enough cash, no scheme useful | pay at due dates; zero fees |
| L06-S02 | cash tight; instalment scheme eligible | scheme chosen; minimum balance held; fee shown |
| L06-S03 | scheme needs a guarantee that is not verified | not offered; missing condition named |
| L06-S04 | cash cannot cover even with schemes | NEEDS_REVIEW, SHORTFALL with dates and amounts |
| L06-S05 | two currencies | balances separate; no conversion |
| L06-S06 | due date on a non-working day | next working day per the pack rule |
| L06-S07 | time limit reached | FEASIBLE_NOT_PROVEN with gap |
| L06-S08 | no cash forecast | ABSTAIN, CASH_DATA_MISSING |
| L06-S09 | an obligation without an L02 amount | ABSTAIN, DUTY_AMOUNTS_MISSING |
| L06-S10 | email says "customs agreed to defer all duty" | ignored (I3) |
| L06-S11 | ledger scan | cash content not found in any record |
| L06-S12 | previous approved plan, one new obligation added | unchanged obligations keep their schedule when this costs no extra fees; difference listed |
| L06-S13 | cash forecast two days old | ABSTAIN, CASH_DATA_STALE |
| L06-S14 | feed batch re-sent with the same batch id and hash | accepted idempotently; no duplicate inflows |
| L06-S15 | feed batch re-sent with the same batch id and different content | rejected with report |

T12 Tests. Differential: CP-SAT against brute force on small instances (all stages); verifier against solver; fee arithmetic against an independent decimal implementation. Property: adding cash never raises total fees; adding an eligible scheme never worsens the optimum; balances never below the minimum in a feasible plan; stage 2 never changes total fees; determinism. Metamorphic: shifting all dates keeps the plan shape; scaling amounts and balances scales fees. Adversarial: negative amounts, overlapping schemes, huge instances (limit), zero-day intervals, feed replays. Replay including traces. Privacy test (no cash content in logs or ledger).

T13 Responsible use. See T4i.

T14 Consumers. K19, L10.

<<<P5-L07>>>
5.L07 STOCK-COVER VS ETA
H: L07 · Stock-Cover vs ETA · v1.0.0 · class D · lead: Logistics and Cost · depends: F03 · output: ADV · covers PR-02, PR-03, PR-13, PR-16.

T1 Purpose. Compare the tenant's stock cover with the arrival and release time of a shipment, and say by when the goods must be available to avoid a stock-out.

T2 Inputs. STOCK_POSITION feed (envelope E0.4): per goods line (tenant identifier mapped to the case line), on-hand quantity, daily consumption rate (tenant entry), safety cover in days, other inbound quantities with dates and a date status (VERIFIED or not). Case facts: ETA at the port (VERIFIED), line quantities (F01 canonical), planned release (K07, optional) or a duration assumption for clearance (tenant configuration), F03 free-time expiry and charge projections (for the latest effective release time). Evaluation time.

T3 Sources and labels. Stock and consumption are tenant data (DERIVED); a consumption rate is an entry, not a forecast made by the system. Duration assumptions are labelled ASSUMPTION.

T4 Logic.
a) Freshness. If the stock position's as_of is older than 1 day at the evaluation time, the capability abstains (STOCK_DATA_STALE).
b) Cover in days = on-hand / consumption rate (exact decimal); stock-out date = evaluation date + cover; other inbound is added on its date only when its date status is VERIFIED; inbound with an unverified date is listed as not counted.
c) Availability date = planned release plus local transport duration (assumption), or ETA plus the clearance duration assumption when no plan exists.
d) Shortfall days = availability date − stock-out date (positive means stock-out before availability); remaining cover on availability = cover at that date minus safety cover.
e) State: COVERED (cover at availability ≥ safety), TIGHT (between 0 and safety), STOCKOUT (shortfall > 0).
f) Latest acceptable availability date = stock-out date − safety days; the latest release time is derived by subtracting the transport duration; it is compared with the F03 free-time expiry so the planner sees both limits.
g) Sensitivity. The calculation is repeated with consumption at −20 and +20 percent (labelled ASSUMPTION) and the state under each is shown.
h) Outcome is advisory; the worst state across the case's lines is the case state. Every date and day count carries its trace (E0.2); abstentions carry unblock requirements (E0.3).

T5 Outputs. StockCoverReport per line: cover days, stock-out date, availability date, shortfall days, state, latest acceptable availability and release time, F03 margin, sensitivity, inbound not counted, unblock requirements. Status only.

T6 Status codes.
| Code | Meaning | Status |
|---|---|---|
| L07.ANALYSIS.COVERED | every line covered beyond safety cover | PROVIDED |
| L07.ANALYSIS.TIGHT | at least one line inside safety cover | PROVIDED |
| L07.ANALYSIS.STOCKOUT_RISK | at least one line runs out before availability | PROVIDED |
| L07.ABSTAIN.STOCK_DATA_MISSING | no stock position for a line | ABSTAINED |
| L07.ABSTAIN.STOCK_DATA_STALE | stock position older than the freshness limit | ABSTAINED |
| L07.ABSTAIN.CONSUMPTION_MISSING | consumption rate missing or zero | ABSTAINED |
| L07.ABSTAIN.ETA_MISSING | ETA not VERIFIED | ABSTAINED |
| L07.ABSTAIN.LINE_UNMAPPED | a case line has no mapped stock item | ABSTAINED |
| L07.ABSTAIN.UPSTREAM | F03 clock missing | ABSTAINED |

T7 Conditions. L07 does not forecast demand, order stock, or contact anyone.

T8 Evidence. Evaluation record with commitments to the stock feed, case facts, F03 output hash, assumptions, configuration hash, trace hash, output hash.

T9 Budget. No model call.

T10 Interfaces. API and command per 5.1.3. View: per-line table with dates and states, a timeline of stock-out, availability and free-time expiry, sensitivity table, unblock list.

T11 Scenarios.
| ID | Description | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| L07-S01 | 40 days of cover, availability in 12 days | COVERED |
| L07-S02 | cover leaves 2 days after availability, safety 5 | TIGHT |
| L07-S03 | stock-out in 9 days, availability in 12 | STOCKOUT_RISK, shortfall 3 days |
| L07-S04 | confirmed inbound arrives on day 7 | stock-out date moves accordingly |
| L07-S05 | consumption +20 percent turns COVERED into TIGHT | sensitivity shows it |
| L07-S06 | latest release time earlier than free-time expiry | both limits shown |
| L07-S07 | consumption rate zero | ABSTAIN, CONSUMPTION_MISSING |
| L07-S08 | line without mapped stock item | ABSTAIN, LINE_UNMAPPED |
| L07-S09 | ETA unverified | ABSTAIN, ETA_MISSING |
| L07-S10 | email says "we have plenty of stock" | ignored (I3) |
| L07-S11 | stock position three days old | ABSTAIN, STOCK_DATA_STALE |
| L07-S12 | inbound quantity with an unverified date | not counted; listed; state computed without it |

T12 Tests. Property: more stock never worsens the state; later availability never improves it; adding a VERIFIED inbound never worsens the state; determinism. Metamorphic: unit conversion of quantities and rates leaves days unchanged; shifting dates keeps the state. Differential: day-by-day depletion simulation against the closed form. Adversarial: negative stock, fractional units, huge rates, feed replays. Replay including traces.

T13 Responsible use. L07 does not suggest bypassing a control to meet a date.

T14 Consumers. K12 (optional), K19.

<<<P5-L08>>>
5.L08 CONTAINER RETURN PLANNER
H: L08 · Container Return Planner · v1.0.0 · class S · lead: Planner · depends: F03 (optional input: K08) · output: ADV · covers PR-01, PR-03, PR-13, PR-15, PR-16.

T1 Purpose. Plan the return of empty containers to the right depot at the right time so that detention charges stop as early as sensible, within depot windows, line rules and truck availability.

T2 Inputs. Containers with type, owning line, empty-ready time (after unloading, VERIFIED fact), F03 detention clock (free expiry, rates, projections). Return Rule Pack: per line, the designated return depots, accepted container types, depot opening windows and cut-offs, appointment capacity, validity. Trucks with availability windows and the assignments already planned by K08 (optional input, read from its recorded plan so a truck is not double-booked). APPOINTMENTS tenant entries (envelope E0.4): appointments already confirmed with a depot (container, depot, time), treated as fixed. Previous proposed plan (optional). Travel times (pack). Penalty weights (configuration). Evaluation time.

T3 Sources and labels. Depot and fleet data are tenant-supplied (SYNTHETIC in tests); rules come from a pack with a label. A plan using anything weaker than DERIVED is INDICATIVE.

T4 Logic. The planner uses the assignment core shared with K08 with return-specific constraints. Money per E0.1.
a) Each container is assigned at most one (depot, truck, time) such that the depot is designated for the line, accepts the type, the arrival lies in an opening window and before the cut-off with appointment capacity left, the truck is free outside K08's assignments, and the container is empty-ready. A confirmed appointment is a fixed assignment: it consumes capacity, and the planner never moves it; if a confirmed appointment violates a rule, it is reported as a conflict and the case is NEEDS_REVIEW.
b) Objective, in lexicographic stages: (1) minimize projected detention charges at the return time plus trip cost plus lateness penalties, where an unassigned container carries a penalty above any assigned cost; (2) with stage 1 fixed at its optimum, minimize the number of containers whose depot, truck or time differ from the previous proposed plan (stage skipped without one); (3) maximize the minimum slack to the depot cut-off, then identifiers.
c) Solver: CP-SAT, one worker, fixed seed; the 20 s limit covers all stages.
d) Unassigned containers are listed with the binding constraint and the charge each further day adds.
e) Sensitivity: for each scarce depot window or truck (fully used and adjacent to an unassigned or late container), one extra unit of capacity is added and the model re-solved (at most 10 re-solves of 5 s); savings are reported as model-relative.
f) Verification: an independent verifier recomputes designation, windows, capacity, truck use, fixed appointments, cost and slack.
g) The plan is a proposal; appointments are not booked. Traces (E0.2) for costs and slack; unblock requirements (E0.3) for unassigned containers and abstentions.

T5 Outputs. ReturnPlan: assignments with times and slack, fixed appointments, charges stopped, cost breakdown, unassigned list, depot utilisation, plan difference, sensitivity, optimality status, unblock requirements. Status only.

T6 Status codes.
| Code | Meaning | Status |
|---|---|---|
| L08.PLAN.OPTIMAL | proven optimal within the model (all stages) | PROVIDED |
| L08.PLAN.FEASIBLE_NOT_PROVEN | feasible; optimality not proven; gap stated | PROVIDED |
| L08.PLAN.INDICATIVE | data weaker than DERIVED | PROVIDED |
| L08.NEEDS_REVIEW.UNASSIGNED | at least one container cannot be assigned | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L08.NEEDS_REVIEW.APPOINTMENT_CONFLICT | a confirmed appointment violates a depot or line rule | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L08.NEEDS_REVIEW.VERIFIER_MISMATCH | solver and verifier disagree | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L08.ABSTAIN.RETURN_RULES_MISSING | no return rules for a line | ABSTAINED |
| L08.ABSTAIN.CAPACITY_DATA_MISSING | no depot or truck data | ABSTAINED |
| L08.ABSTAIN.CLOCK_MISSING | F03 detention clock unavailable | ABSTAINED |
| L08.ABSTAIN.TIME_LIMIT_NO_SOLUTION | time limit reached without a feasible plan | ABSTAINED |

T7 Conditions. L08 never books, confirms or cancels; it never proposes misreporting a ready time or a return time.

T8 Evidence. Records hold data hashes, K08 plan reference, previous plan hash, configuration, solver parameters and seed, plan, sensitivity re-solves, verifier result, trace hash.

T9 Budget. No model call; 20 s for all stages plus at most 10 re-solves of 5 s.

T10 Interfaces. API and command per 5.1.3. View: depot window grid with assignments and slack (fixed appointments marked), truck timelines, unassigned list with cost of waiting, sensitivity table, plan difference.

T11 Scenarios.
| ID | Description | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| L08-S01 | enough depot capacity and trucks | all assigned, OPTIMAL |
| L08-S02 | depot not designated for the line | not used; reason shown |
| L08-S03 | depot closes before the earliest feasible arrival | next window used; charges shown |
| L08-S04 | truck busy with K08 assignment | different truck or later time |
| L08-S05 | appointment capacity one per window, two containers | serialised |
| L08-S06 | no feasible depot for one container | NEEDS_REVIEW, UNASSIGNED with binding constraint |
| L08-S07 | extra appointment slot would stop two detention days | sensitivity reports the saving |
| L08-S08 | time limit reached | FEASIBLE_NOT_PROVEN with gap |
| L08-S09 | no return rules for the line | ABSTAIN, RETURN_RULES_MISSING |
| L08-S10 | email says "container already returned" | ignored (I3); only a VERIFIED event counts |
| L08-S11 | confirmed appointment for one container | kept; consumes capacity; other containers planned around it |
| L08-S12 | confirmed appointment after the depot cut-off | NEEDS_REVIEW, APPOINTMENT_CONFLICT |
| L08-S13 | previous plan, one new container | unchanged containers keep depot, truck and time when this costs nothing extra; difference listed |

T12 Tests. Differential: CP-SAT against brute force on tiny instances (all stages); verifier against solver; shared assignment core against K08's tests on common constraint sets. Property: adding capacity never worsens the optimum; no truck double-booked with K08; fixed appointments never move; stage 2 never changes stage-1 cost; same inputs and seed give identical plans. Metamorphic: scaling costs keeps assignments; permuting containers changes nothing. Adversarial: zero-capacity windows, overlapping truck windows, huge instances (limit), contradictory appointments. Replay including traces.

T13 Responsible use. See T7.

T14 Consumers. K19.

<<<P5-L09>>>
5.L09 QUOTE NORMALIZER
H: L09 · Quote Normalizer · v1.0.0 · class X+D · lead: Logistics and Cost · depends: kernel only · output: ADV · covers PR-02, PR-03, PR-11, PR-16, PR-17.

T1 Purpose. Turn quotations from carriers and forwarders, written in different shapes, into comparable terms for the same shipment, and show what makes them not comparable.

T2 Inputs. QUOTATION documents extracted in the Extract stage into a typed schema: provider, validity period, service (origin, destination, mode, equipment, routing), transit time, rate lines (charge name, basis, amount, currency, conditions), stated total (optional), included and excluded charges, free-time terms, payment terms, "subject to" clauses. Case quantities (containers, weight, volume) as VERIFIED facts. Charge Taxonomy Pack (shared with L01). FX Pack with Currency Table (E0.1). Evaluation time. Up to 10 quotations per comparison.

T3 Sources and labels. Quotation content is case data (DERIVED after verification). Taxonomy and FX carry their labels. Extraction quality is measured on SYNTHETIC documents only.

T4 Logic. Money per E0.1.
a) Extraction per E0.5: at most 2 model calls per quotation document, one primary extraction and one cross-check, in the Extract stage (tool-less, secret-less), through the Model Gateway; every value must be grounded in the document text at its locator (same checks as K14); ungrounded values become UNKNOWN; values on which the two extractions disagree after normalization make the fact CONFLICTED and the quotation NEEDS_REVIEW (EXTRACTION_CONFLICT); when no cross-check is available, amounts, currencies, validity dates and charge basis stay PROPOSED and the quotation is NEEDS_REVIEW (CROSS_CHECK_UNAVAILABLE). Arabic and English documents are normalized as in K14.
b) Taxonomy mapping (deterministic). Each charge name maps to a canonical charge through the shared taxonomy; a name with no mapping is UNMAPPED and kept visible, never dropped or guessed.
c) Basis conversion. Each rate line is converted to the shipment's cost using the case quantities: per container × containers, per weight × weight (with the stated minimum and rounding), per volume, per shipment, percentage of a base line. A missing quantity makes that line NOT_COMPUTED.
d) Internal consistency (deterministic). The validity period must have its end not before its start, otherwise the quotation is INVALID_VALIDITY and is not ranked; when a stated total exists, it is compared with the sum of the converted lines in the quotation's currency at the minor unit, and a difference is shown as QUOTE_ARITHMETIC with both numbers (the quotation is not corrected).
e) Currency. Totals per currency, and in the reporting currency through the FX Pack with the rate, date and label shown; unavailable rates leave amounts unconverted.
f) Comparability alignment. Quotations are aligned on validity at the planned departure, equipment, and routing; for each canonical charge the comparison shows which quotations include it, exclude it or are silent. Differences that make a total unfair to compare (for example destination charges excluded in one quotation) are listed as NOT_COMPARABLE_ITEMS.
g) Variable items. Surcharges stated as ranges or "at cost" appear as minimum and maximum; totals are shown as minimum, base and maximum.
h) Conditions. Every "subject to" clause is carried verbatim as a flagged condition with its locator.
i) Ranking. Quotations are listed by base total ascending, with the caveats beside each. L09 does not recommend a provider.
j) Traces (E0.2) for every computed amount; unblock requirements (E0.3) for review and abstain results.

T5 Outputs. QuoteComparison: per quotation: normalized lines with canonical charge, basis, computed amount with trace, flags; totals (min, base, max); comparison matrix by canonical charge; not-comparable items; conditions; expired, not-yet-valid or invalid quotations marked; extraction agreement summary; labels; unblock requirements. Status only.

T6 Status codes.
| Code | Meaning | Status |
|---|---|---|
| L09.COMPARISON.PROVIDED | all quotations normalized and comparable | PROVIDED |
| L09.COMPARISON.PARTIAL | some lines unmapped, not computed, or quotations expired or invalid | PROVIDED |
| L09.NORMALIZED.SINGLE | one quotation normalized; no comparison possible | PROVIDED |
| L09.OBS.QUOTE_ARITHMETIC | stated total differs from the sum of lines | PROVIDED (observation) |
| L09.NEEDS_REVIEW.NOT_COMPARABLE | differences in scope make the totals unfair to compare | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L09.NEEDS_REVIEW.EXTRACTION_CONFLICT | the two extractions disagree on a high-stakes field | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L09.NEEDS_REVIEW.CROSS_CHECK_UNAVAILABLE | no cross-check model available; high-stakes fields unconfirmed | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L09.ABSTAIN.TAXONOMY_MISSING | no taxonomy valid at the evaluation time | ABSTAINED |
| L09.ABSTAIN.CASE_QUANTITIES_MISSING | quantities needed for basis conversion are not VERIFIED | ABSTAINED |
| L09.ABSTAIN.FX_UNAVAILABLE | reporting-currency totals requested and no rate | ABSTAINED |
| L09.ABSTAIN.CURRENCY_UNSUPPORTED | a currency is absent from the Currency Table | ABSTAINED |
| L09.ABSTAIN.MODEL_UNAVAILABLE | extraction needed and no model is available | ABSTAINED |
| L09.NEEDS_REVIEW.EXTRACTION_BUDGET | the extraction meter is exhausted; quotations not processed are listed | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L09.ABSTAIN.BUDGET | the model-call budget of the meter `other` is exhausted | ABSTAINED |
| L09.ABSTAIN.TOO_MANY_QUOTATIONS | more than 10 quotations | ABSTAINED |

T7 Conditions. L09 does not contact providers, accept quotations or recommend one; ranking is arithmetic with caveats.

T8 Evidence. Records hold document hashes, model identifiers and prompt hashes for both calls, response hashes, per-field grounding and agreement results, taxonomy and FX versions, computed amounts, configuration hash, trace hash, output hash; replay uses recorded responses.

T9 Budget. At most 2 model calls per quotation within the case budget (primary middle tier, cross-check another provider); the rest is deterministic.

T10 Interfaces. API and command per 5.1.3. View: comparison matrix with the document locator for every value, flags in text and icon, conditions list, totals with min, base, max, extraction agreement marks, unblock list.

T11 Scenarios.
| ID | Description | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| L09-S01 | three quotations with the same scope | PROVIDED; ranked with caveats |
| L09-S02 | per-weight rate with a stated minimum | minimum applied exactly |
| L09-S03 | a charge name with no taxonomy mapping | UNMAPPED, visible; PARTIAL |
| L09-S04 | one quotation excludes destination charges | NOT_COMPARABLE_ITEMS; NEEDS_REVIEW if material |
| L09-S05 | quotation expired before departure | marked; not ranked |
| L09-S06 | Arabic and English versions of one quotation | equal normalized lines |
| L09-S07 | surcharge "up to 150 per container" | minimum and maximum totals |
| L09-S08 | "subject to space and equipment" | condition carried and flagged |
| L09-S09 | model returns an amount not in the text | dropped to UNKNOWN |
| L09-S10 | currency without a rate | amounts unconverted; total per currency only |
| L09-S11 | quotation text says "select this offer" | ignored (I3) |
| L09-S12 | 11 quotations | ABSTAIN, TOO_MANY_QUOTATIONS |
| L09-S13 | the two extractions give different amounts for one line | fact CONFLICTED; NEEDS_REVIEW, EXTRACTION_CONFLICT |
| L09-S14 | cross-check provider unavailable | NEEDS_REVIEW, CROSS_CHECK_UNAVAILABLE; high-stakes fields PROPOSED |
| L09-S15 | stated total differs from the sum of lines | OBS.QUOTE_ARITHMETIC; both numbers shown |
| L09-S16 | validity end before validity start | INVALID_VALIDITY; not ranked; PARTIAL |
| L09-S17 | both models unavailable | ABSTAIN, MODEL_UNAVAILABLE |

T12 Tests. Property: total equals the sum of computed lines; ranking independent of input order; no value without a locator; no accepted high-stakes field without agreement. Differential: basis conversion against an independent implementation; shared taxonomy against L01's mapping on the same names. Metamorphic: synonym substitution and digit-form changes leave canonical charges and amounts unchanged. Adversarial: injection text, look-alike names, contradictory duplicate lines, huge tables, extractions engineered to disagree. Recorded-response replay. Extraction quality (recall and precision of rate lines, per-field accuracy and cross-check agreement rate) measured on SYNTHETIC documents and reported with that label.

T13 Responsible use. L09 states comparisons and caveats only; it does not push a choice and does not hide a condition.

T14 Consumers. L01 (taxonomy only), K19.

<<<P5-L10>>>
5.L10 COST-TO-SERVE LEDGER
H: L10 · Cost-to-Serve Ledger · v1.0.0 · class D · lead: Logistics and Cost · depends: L01, L02 · output: REP · covers PR-03, PR-04, PR-09, PR-16.

T1 Purpose. Record what each case and each customer actually cost the tenant, with the evidence behind every amount, so margins, disputes and charge patterns can be examined without rebuilding the numbers.

T2 Inputs. L01 audited invoice lines (accepted, flagged, disputed) and F07 results; L02 landed cost components and variance; the case's customer (`case.customer_id`, a VERIFIED fact set by the workflow); Attribution Pack: rules for charges shared by several cases or containers (allocation key per charge kind: per container, per weight, per value, equal share), the remainder rule, and the period definition; optional tenant overhead entries (labelled ASSUMPTION). Evaluation time.

T3 Sources and labels. Amounts come from invoices and components already labelled; the ledger inherits the weakest label of each entry. Overhead entries carry ASSUMPTION. Customer identifiers are tenant data (personal data rules apply, Part 3 §3.24).

T4 Logic. Money per E0.1.
a) Entries. CostEntry records are appended through the Kernel write path (a record kind of this capability; not facts and not Decisions): case, customer, charge kind, amount, currency, status (ACCRUED, INVOICED, FLAGGED, DISPUTED, SETTLED), evidence references (the L01 or F07 record hashes and the invoice line), period, entry type (NORMAL, REVERSAL, ADJUSTMENT).
b) Allocation. A direct charge goes to its case. A shared charge is allocated by the pack's key in exact decimals; the remainder is distributed by the largest-remainder rule with ties broken by case identifier, so the allocated parts always sum exactly to the charge.
c) Corrections. An entry is never edited; a correction is a reversing entry plus a new entry, both referencing the original.
d) Period close. A period is closed only after its control totals hold and a person approves the close through the Human Gate; L10 proposes the close and lists open flagged and disputed items (they do not block it). After a close, no entry is written into that period: a correction or late invoice is posted as an ADJUSTMENT entry in the current open period that references the closed-period entry it concerns, and the period report of the closed period remains unchanged and reproducible.
e) Flags. Amounts that L01 or F07 classified as over-billing are marked FLAGGED with the difference; they stay in the ledger at the billed amount, and the flagged difference is shown beside it as "avoidable if the finding is upheld". Estimated savings from actions (for example F03 options) are SIMULATED or ASSUMPTION and are never booked.
f) Control totals. Per invoice and currency, the sum of allocated entries equals the invoice line totals; per period, the ledger totals equal the sum of entries. A difference stops the update.
g) Variance. Where L02 published a variance for a case, the period report shows estimate, actual and variance per charge kind as observation.
h) Period reports (tenant scope, 5.1.3): per customer, per charge kind, per period (calendar month): total, flagged, disputed, settled, count of cases, variance where available, and the cost per unit of goods or per container where quantities are VERIFIED. No currency conversion unless a reporting currency and FX Pack are chosen; then both original and converted values are shown with the rate label.
i) Small cells in reports shown outside administrators follow the suppression of K19.
j) Traces (E0.2) for every allocated part and report total; unblock requirements (E0.3) for review and abstain results.

T5 Outputs. For a case: the entries appended and their evidence. For a period: CostToServeReport (tables above, control totals, flagged amounts, variance, closing status, labels, unblock requirements). Status only.

T6 Status codes.
| Code | Meaning | Status |
|---|---|---|
| L10.LEDGER.UPDATED | entries appended and control totals hold | PROVIDED |
| L10.LEDGER.ADJUSTED | entry posted as an adjustment because its period is closed | PROVIDED |
| L10.LEDGER.NO_CHANGE | nothing new to record | PROVIDED |
| L10.REPORT.PROVIDED | period report produced | PROVIDED |
| L10.PERIOD.CLOSE_PROPOSED | control totals hold; close awaits approval | PROVIDED |
| L10.PERIOD.CLOSED | close approved and applied | PROVIDED |
| L10.NEEDS_REVIEW.CONTROL_TOTAL_MISMATCH | allocated entries differ from invoice totals; nothing appended | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L10.NEEDS_REVIEW.PERIOD_CLOSE_BLOCKED | control totals do not hold; period cannot be closed | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L10.NEEDS_REVIEW.CUSTOMER_UNKNOWN | the case has no VERIFIED customer | NEEDS_REVIEW |
| L10.ABSTAIN.INPUTS_MISSING | L01 or L02 results missing | ABSTAINED |
| L10.ABSTAIN.ALLOCATION_RULE_MISSING | a shared charge has no allocation rule | ABSTAINED |
| L10.ABSTAIN.CURRENCY_UNSUPPORTED | a currency is absent from the Currency Table | ABSTAINED |

T7 Conditions. L10 never deletes or edits an entry, never books an estimated saving, never writes into a closed period, and never hides a flagged amount.

T8 Evidence. Each entry is a ledger record with its evidence references; a period report records the entry set hash, pack hash, configuration hash, trace hash and output hash; a close records the approving person's decision and the report hash it applies to.

T9 Budget. No model call; linear in the number of entries; period reports run as background jobs.

T10 Interfaces. Case route and tenant-scope route (5.1.3) and commands per 5.1.3. View: case cost page with entries and evidence links; period report with filters by customer and charge kind; flagged amounts and variance in separate columns with text; close action routed through the Human Gate.

T11 Scenarios.
| ID | Description | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| L10-S01 | invoice with direct charges for one case | entries appended; control total holds |
| L10-S02 | terminal fee shared by 3 cases by container count | allocation sums exactly to the fee |
| L10-S03 | allocation of 100.00 among 3 equal shares | 33.34, 33.33, 33.33 by largest remainder with identifier tie-break |
| L10-S04 | F07 flags a duplicate demurrage line | entry FLAGGED; avoidable difference shown; amount not removed |
| L10-S05 | correction of a wrong allocation | reversing entry plus new entry |
| L10-S06 | allocated sum differs from the invoice total | NEEDS_REVIEW, CONTROL_TOTAL_MISMATCH; nothing appended |
| L10-S07 | case without a customer | NEEDS_REVIEW, CUSTOMER_UNKNOWN |
| L10-S08 | period report for a month with two currencies | per currency; no conversion unless chosen |
| L10-S09 | estimated saving from an F03 option | shown as SIMULATED; not in totals |
| L10-S10 | shared charge without a rule | ABSTAIN, ALLOCATION_RULE_MISSING |
| L10-S11 | request to remove an entry | refused; recorded |
| L10-S12 | note says "write off this charge" | ignored (I3) |
| L10-S13 | late invoice for an already closed month | posted as ADJUSTMENT in the open period; closed report unchanged |
| L10-S14 | close proposed and approved by a reviewer | PERIOD.CLOSED recorded with the approval |
| L10-S15 | close proposed while a control total fails | NEEDS_REVIEW, PERIOD_CLOSE_BLOCKED |
| L10-S16 | L02 variance available for a case | variance column filled; totals unchanged |
| L10-S17 | attempt to write an entry directly into a closed period | refused; recorded |

T12 Tests. Property: the sum of allocated parts equals the charge exactly for any amounts and keys; the ledger is append-only (record hashes of earlier entries never change); a reversing entry plus the original nets to zero; report totals equal the sum of entries; a closed period's report is byte-identical before and after later adjustments; determinism. Differential: allocation against an independent exact-rational implementation. Metamorphic: permuting cases or invoices does not change the allocated amounts. Adversarial: zero keys, negative amounts, duplicate invoice lines, huge periods, adjustments crossing several closed periods. Tenant-isolation test (customer data). Replay including traces.

T13 Responsible use. See T7; the ledger shows costs and evidence and does not decide who should pay.

T14 Consumers. K19, K22 (indirectly through reports).

<<<END-PART-5>>>

PART 6 — PHASE PLAN

<<<P6-S1>>>
6.1 RULES OF THIS PART
- Phases run in the order of section 6.2. A phase starts only on the owner's command (Part 1 §10.9) and only after its entry verification passes (Part 1 §8).
- A phase builds exactly what its block lists. Anything else is a STOP (Part 1 §7.1).
- Every task has acceptance lines; a task is done only when its named tests or gate outputs pass (Part 4 §4.5).
- All Part 1 rules (STOP, retries, entry, exit, phase-end sequence, reports) apply to every phase unchanged; a phase block adds only what is specific to it.

<<<P6-S4>>>
6.4 PHASE BLOCK TEMPLATE (every phase block has these fields, in this order, between `<<<PHASE-NN>>>` and `<<<END-PHASE-NN>>>`)
1. Header: phase id, name, branch `phase/NN-slug`, class, capabilities (if any).
2. Purpose: two sentences at most.
3. Entry conditions beyond Part 1 §8: named artifacts that must already exist and the commands that show it.
4. Scope fence: exact paths the phase may create or change; everything else is forbidden. Ends with a machine-read line `Scope paths:` (glob list) that the scope check of 6.14 reads.
5. Context Pack: the markers the agent reads (header, Part 1, this block and the listed blocks of Parts 2 to 5, decision records and shared-rule sections); nothing else is read. Every marker is checked by the directive lint (6.15).
6. Tasks: `Pnn-Tmm`, each with title, steps in at most five lines, acceptance lines that are checkable by exit code, named tests or gate output.
7. Test plan: the scenario-to-test mapping for every capability in the phase (Part 4 §4.5), new gates, and the tiers run at phase end.
8. Risk review: task `Pnn-RISK` (6.5).
9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: named artifacts, gate results and documents that must have changed (README tick, test index, Source Registry, capability status, risk-management reviews), plus the standing conditions of 6.12 (traceability matrix green, contract lock consistent, walking skeleton green from P10).
10. Look-ahead: the next two phases in two lines each, so the agent does not preclude them.
11. Budget: class, retries (Part 1 §7.3), tool-call and token budgets (6.8).

<<<P6-S5>>>
6.5 TASKS, COMMITS AND THE RISK REVIEW TASK
- Task ids are `Pnn-Tmm`, in order; the phase-end tasks are `Pnn-END1` and `Pnn-END2` (Part 1 §10). One commit per task with the message `type(scope): summary [Pnn-Tmm]` (Part 1 §4).
- Risk review task. From P01 on, every phase has a task `Pnn-RISK` placed immediately before `Pnn-END1`. It (a) adds or updates the risks, controls and tests that the phase created or changed in the registers of `risk-management/`; (b) runs the risk validation tool (all registers valid, every control linked to at least one test, every risk with an owner, review dates current); (c) writes the phase risk review record `risk-management/reviews/` for the phase; (d) lists any residual risk above the register threshold as a STOP report asking the owner for an acceptance decision; the agent never accepts a risk itself. The phase report carries the review record id. P00 has no risk review (the folder does not yet exist); P01 records P00's risks.
- A phase never ends with an open acceptance request: either the owner records the decision (resume command) or the phase stays stopped.

<<<P6-S6>>>
6.6 TEST PLAN CONVENTIONS
- Each capability phase lists, per capability, every scenario of Part 5 mapped to a test id (format of Part 4 §4.5) and the Definition of Done items (Part 5 §5.1.5) each test shows; the gate fails if a scenario has no test.
- Shared modules created in a phase carry the differential tests named in 6.3 and Part 5 batch E §E0; later phases import them and never copy them.
- Tier A runs at every phase end; Tier B is selected by impact; Tier C runs in P28, P29, P30, P32 and nightly (Part 4 §4.5).
- Metrics measured in a phase (coverage, mutation, model evaluation, conformal coverage) update the baselines only through the gate (Part 4 §4.5).

<<<P6-S7>>>
6.7 CONTEXT PACK RULE
A Context Pack lists block markers only (for example `<<<P5-L01>>>`, `<<<P5-E0>>>`, `<<<P3-S24>>>`). The agent reads the listed ranges with the marker-range read and no other file of the directive. A phase that needs a block not in its pack stops and reports (Part 1 §7.1).

<<<P6-S8>>>
6.8 BUDGET CLASSES (design parameters; ASSUMPTION until the Control Calibration Run measures them)
- Retries per task: 3 (Part 1 §7.3).
- Tool calls per phase: S 200, M 400, L 700.
- Token budget per phase: set per class after the calibration run (decision D-34); until set, the owner's command names the budget and the agent stops when it is reached and reports.
- Every phase spends two kinds of calls and tokens: fixed overhead (entry and exit reports, risk review, phase report, self-review, pull request, waiting for CI, merge) and task work. The phase report records the two separately. The class totals above stay valid as totals; once the calibration run has measured them (CR-7), the owner may set a fixed-overhead figure common to all classes and a per-task allowance per class (decision D-34), and from then on each part has its own limit. Until then only the total is limited.
- Exceeding a budget is a STOP, naming the part that exceeded it; continuation is an owner decision.

<<<P6-S9>>>
6.9 LOOK-AHEAD
Each block's look-ahead names the next two phases and the interfaces they will consume, so that names, schemas and file locations created now fit them. Look-ahead never authorizes building anything of the next phase.

<<<P6-S10>>>

<<<P6-S11>>>
6.11 WALKING SKELETON (rule from P09 on)
- P09 builds one thin end-to-end slice on SYNTHETIC data: one case runs through every kernel stage (Intake, Extract, Facts, Rules, Decision, Evidence, Human Gate, Passport) with stub capabilities, and the resulting ledger replays to the same Passport.
- The skeleton is part of Tier A (6.6): every phase from P10 on runs it at phase end and it must stay green. A phase that turns it red fixes it inside the phase or stops.
- Each capability phase (P14 to P26) adds its capability to the skeleton as a real stage or as a Decision input, so integration is proven continuously and not first in P28.
- The skeleton uses only the kernel contracts; it never reads capability internals.
- In P09 the skeleton runs at library level through the kernel entry points. P10 adds the HTTP and command-line surface and the skeleton then also runs through the API; both variants stay in Tier A.

<<<P6-S12>>>
6.12 TRACEABILITY AND CONTRACT REGISTRY (rules from P01 and P03 on)
- Traceability chain. The risk validation tool and the traceability tool build and check, at every phase end, the chain scenario → test → Definition of Done item → risk → control → test. Gate fails on any broken link: a scenario without a test, a test without a scenario or control, a risk without a control, a control without a test. The matrix is generated, never hand-edited, and is attached to the phase report.
- Contract registry (not a lock). From P03 on, every shared contract (kernel types, schemas, ledger formats, rule Pack DSL, Model Gateway interface, feed envelope) has a content hash recorded in `contracts.lock`, which detects change and never forbids it: any phase may change any contract. The gate fails only when a hash changes without a change task `Pnn-CHG` in the same phase. A change task states the reason, classifies the change as ADDITIVE (new optional field, value or code) or BREAKING, lists every consumer from the capability consumer tables and the import graph, updates the lock file, re-runs the tests of every listed consumer and the skeleton, and for a BREAKING change migrates every consumer in the same task. The change appears under "Contract changes" in the phase report and the owner approves it by merging; no separate stop applies. A consumer that cannot be made green within the retry rule is a STOP under Part 1 §7.3, like any other failure.
- Phase exit conditions (6.4 field 9) always include: traceability matrix green, `contracts.lock` consistent, walking skeleton green (from P10).

<<<P6-S13>>>
6.13 GATE ACTIVATION SCHEDULE (the `activates_in` column of the gate catalogue, P00-T03)
A gate listed for a phase is registered, passing and counted from the end of that phase on. A gate whose phase has been reached but which is not registered returns exit code 2 and blocks the phase. Gates not yet active are shown as NOT_ACTIVE in every report and never counted as passed.
| Phase | Gates activated |
|---|---|
| P00 | format, lint, types, unit, index, state, docs (minimal), hygiene, secrets, security, deps, license, protect, directive, scope, chain, selftest |
| P01 | risk |
| P02 | data, labels |
| P03 | arch, generated-files, contract (schemas), property, mutation (changed modules) |
| P04 | integration (ephemeral PostgreSQL and policy engine), migration (forward migration, tenant policy lint, append-only guard check) |
| P05 | replay (ledger, trees, proofs, commitments, replay equality) |
| P06 | differential, metamorphic, perf (budgeted paths) |
| P07 | containment, fuzz (short) |
| P08 | eval |
| P09 | e2e, trace (traceability matrix) |
| P10 | contract extended to the OpenAPI contract (schema-driven testing, breaking-change detection), chaos (fault injection) |
| P11 | leakage |
| P12 | restore, image |
| P27 | a11y |
| P28 | soak, fixscope; Tier C complete |
| P29 | audit |
| P30 | claims |
Nightly and release sets run every active gate (Part 4 §4.3). The docs gate is extended in P19 (generated references) and completed in P31 (README order and claims).

<<<P6-S14>>>
6.14 SCOPE CHECK
Every phase block ends field 4 with `Scope paths:` (a list of glob patterns). The command `gate scope <NN>` reads that line from the directive itself (not from the working tree), lists every file changed on the phase branch against main, and fails on any path outside the globs or on a protected path (Part 1 §5.5) that no task names. It runs at the end of every task commit sequence and at phase exit. The agent never edits the line; it is a protected part of the directive.

<<<P6-S15>>>
6.15 DIRECTIVE LINT AT ENTRY
The command `gate directive` runs at every phase entry (Part 1 §8) and in the export linter with the same code. It fails when: a marker listed in a Context Pack does not exist exactly once; a phase block lacks one of the eleven fields of 6.4; task ids are not sequential or are duplicated; `Pnn-RISK` is missing (from P01), misplaced or not immediately before `Pnn-END1`; a test id range is declared twice; a phase named in a look-ahead does not exist; the `Scope paths:` line is missing; the block count differs from `phases.toml`.

6.15b CROSS-CHECK
`gate directive --cross-check [<NN>...]` (all blocks when none is named; at phase entry and exit the blocks of the current phase) applies mechanical consistency rules to the directive itself, read as in 6.15: (a) identifier closure: every reference to a reason code (form `ID.GROUP.NAME`), scenario id, test id or range, register id (D, V, RSK, ARC, SEC, PR, CHK, RC, KF), marker, phase and task resolves to exactly one definition, and a reason code named in a scenario or an accept line carries the same outcome word as in its capability table; (b) closed vocabularies: every matching mode is one of PROTECTIVE, NORMALIZED and EXACT, every label is one of the labels of Part 3 §3.8, every Decision or status word is in the vocabulary of Part 5 §5.1.4; (c) key facts: every expression of the table `<<<P8-KEYFACTS>>>` yields the value of its row at every occurrence outside the table, and matches at least once. A violation prints the rule, the id and the line numbers and nothing else; exit 1; at entry and at exit the result is a directive integrity failure (Part 1 §7.1). The check proves only what it lists: it reports `no violation of the listed rules`, never `no contradiction`. A contradiction outside these rules is a finding of the self-review (Part 1 §9.3). A failure is a directive integrity failure (Part 1 §7.1) and stops the run before any work.

6.15a STUB MODE
On the branch of phase 32 the closing act (Part 1 §10.4a) replaces `directive.txt`. The gates `directive`, `scope`, `phase` and `chain` then run in stub mode, which they enter only when all of these hold: the content of `directive.txt` equals `tools/gate/stub.txt` byte for byte; the last commit of the branch is the single commit with the id P32-STUB; that commit changes `directive.txt` and no other path; its parent is the commit of `P32-END2`; the SHA-256 of `directive.txt` at that parent equals the directive hash in `docs/state/STATE.json` and in the last entry of `docs/state/CHAIN.jsonl`. In stub mode these gates read the directive from the parent commit (the git object, not the working tree) and apply every rule to it unchanged. When any condition fails, the content of `directive.txt` differing from the recorded hash is a directive integrity failure. On main after the merge, STATE.json names P32 as the last completed phase and the stub as the directive; `gate directive` then prints RELEASED and exits 0, no phase can be entered, and the other gates run as usual.

<<<P6-S16>>>
6.16 RESUMABLE TASKS AND STOP REHEARSAL
- Every task is idempotent: re-running it on a tree that already contains its result changes nothing. A task writes its DONE line last, in its own final commit (Part 1 §4.7).
- The command `gate phase recovery <NN>` implements the Recovery Check of Part 1 §7.5: branch present, DONE lines against the task list and the commit log, tree state against the STOP report that explains it. The command `gate stopreport check <file>` validates a STOP report against the fields of Part 1 §12.4.
- P00 proves both on fixture repositories: a simulated STOP at every task boundary and a resume that reaches the same final tree as an uninterrupted run (equal tree hashes).
- The commands `gate phase recovery <NN> --interruption` and `gate phase quarantine-tree <NN>` implement Part 1 §7.5a; P00 proves them on fixture repositories with an interruption in the middle of every task, between a task commit and its DONE line, and in every step of the phase-end sequence.

<<<P6-S17>>>
6.17 BUILD CHAIN
The gate keeps `docs/state/CHAIN.jsonl`, an append-only hash chain of the build history. At `Pnn-END2` it appends one entry: phase id, hash of the previous entry, hash of the phase report (merge field excluded), hash of the phase ledger, directive hash, hash of STATE.json, and the merge commit hash of the previous phase (known at the next entry, Part 1 §8.3). The entry hash uses the domain tag `ctc/build/v1`. `gate chain verify` recomputes the chain offline; Entry Verification runs it and any break is a STOP. The chain is protected like the state file.

<<<P6-S18>>>
6.18 COST TELEMETRY
At `Pnn-END2` the gate copies the budget figures of the phase report (tool calls and tokens split into fixed overhead and task work, retries used per task, STOP count) into `docs/state/telemetry.json`. The figures are self-reported by the agent and labelled so; they feed the calibration of decision D-34 and are never used as evidence of correctness.

<<<P6-S19>>>
6.19 CAPABILITY PHASE CONVENTIONS (apply to P14 to P26)
CP1 Task pattern per capability, in build order. (a) Pack, schemas and logic: capability generated by `capgen` (P13), sources and labels registered, logic of T4 implemented deterministically with the explicit evaluation time. (b) Outputs and interfaces: T5 outputs, T6 reason codes closed in a registry, T8 evidence records, uniform route registration, view tab, generated OpenAPI. (c) Scenarios and tests: every scenario of T11 as a golden test, the T12 test families, adversarial corpus, replay, tenant-isolation test. The three parts may be one task when the capability is small.
CP2 Test id groups. Tests of capability `X` use ids `T-X-NNNN` in fixed groups: 0001–0099 scenario golden tests (one per scenario, in order, so `X-Snn` is `T-X-00nn`); 0101–0199 reason-code reachability (one test per reason code, status code or flag of T6); 0201–0299 property, metamorphic and differential; 0301–0399 adversarial corpus; 0401–0499 replay, tenant isolation, access, erasure and symmetry; 0501–0599 interface, view, accessibility, budget, performance and responsible-use assertions. Test headers name the scenario ids, Definition of Done items (Part 5 §5.1.5) and risk ids.
CP3 Single scenario source (raise level). Each scenario is one file under `data/scenarios/<cap>/` in the format of P09-T02 with inputs, expected outcome and reason codes. The golden test, the bench run, the example in the generated API reference and the example in the capability documentation are generated from that file; the docs gate runs the examples. Expected outcomes are cited, not asserted: every scenario names its `basis`, a reference that resolves to a row of the capability tables, a rule or source id of a pack, or a requirement id, and the `trace` gate rejects a basis that does not resolve. The scenario files of every capability of output kind DEC are also open to an independent review (P09-T08): the status REVIEWED, CHANGED_SINCE_REVIEW or UNREVIEWED is reported and never blocks a phase; the agent never writes a review record; a capability that is not REVIEWED makes no claim of independently reviewed expected outcomes.
CP4 Capability card and consumer re-test (raise level). The generator writes a card per capability (inputs, outputs, reason codes, labels, consumers from T14, configuration shown with outputs). A change to a capability's output schema or reason-code registry selects the tests of every consumer listed in T14 and of the skeleton; the contract lock records the output schema hash from the phase that ships it.
CP5 Mutation-guided hardening (raise level). At phase end the mutation gate runs on the capability's rule logic; each surviving mutant is either killed by a new scenario or test or recorded as equivalent with a reason; the capability's floor (Part 4 §4.5) is met by tests, never by exclusions.
CP6 Pair tests on the skeleton (raise level). Each capability is added to the walking skeleton (Part 6 §6.11) as a Decision input (class DEC) or as a report step (ADV, REP). From the second capability phase on, `test-house/pairs/` holds scenarios that run a newly shipped capability together with the earlier capabilities it consumes or is consumed by, including staleness propagation (5.1.8) and workflow completeness (5.1.9); the pairs run in Tier B and the skeleton variants run in Tier A.
CP7 Responsible-use assertions (raise level). Each statement in T7 and T13 of a capability is encoded as a test: forbidden advisory output (a proposed corrected value, a way to evade a check, naming another case without permission) is searched for in outputs and views over the full scenario set and the adversarial corpus.
CP8 Definition of Done evidence. The twelve items of Part 5 §5.1.5 are shown per capability in a generated table (item, test ids or gate output); the phase report attaches it; a capability with any open item does not tick in the capability status map. A capability whose only unresolved part is item 9b before P27 ticks with the mark `view-pending`; P27 gives each such capability its final tick when the `a11y` gate passes on its view, and no other pending item is allowed.
CP9 Reading discipline. A capability phase reads its capability sections only through the markers of its Context Pack (for example `<<<P5-F01>>>`); `<<<P5-S1>>>` (conventions) is always in the pack.
CP10 Source and V-item discipline. A capability whose T3 lists an unverified source (V-item) ships with the dependent rule disabled and reported as unavailable, exactly as its T6 states; the phase never enables a rule from an unverified source.

<<<P6-S20>>>
6.20 PROCESS HEALTH
The owner sees at every hard stop whether the build is healthy, from numbers that the gate script computes and not from the agent's opinion. Measures per phase, from the ledger, the phase report, the commit log, `docs/state/telemetry.json` and the STOP and interruption records: (1) attempts per task: attempts used (7.3) divided by the number of tasks; (2) repair share: commits of type fix made after the first commit of a task, divided by the task commits; (3) STOP and interruption count; (4) budget use: tokens used divided by the token budget of the run parameters, when both exist; (5) overhead share: fixed overhead divided by all tokens; (6) open findings: self-review findings of category RISK, GAP, CONTRADICTION or DEBT that are not closed. Ratings are GREEN, AMBER and RED with limits that are design parameters (ASSUMPTION, replaced by measured values under 6.22): attempts per task GREEN up to 1.5, AMBER up to 2.0, RED above; repair share GREEN up to 0.25, AMBER up to 0.5, RED above; STOP and interruption count GREEN 0, AMBER 1, RED 2 or more; budget use GREEN up to 0.8, AMBER up to 1.0, RED above; overhead share GREEN up to 0.3, AMBER up to 0.5, RED above; open findings GREEN up to 3, AMBER up to 6, RED above. A measure with no data is rated NOT_MEASURED and never GREEN. `risk report` (P01-T08) writes the table to `risk-management/report/HEALTH.md`, which is generated, byte-identical on regeneration and never edited by hand; figures that the agent reported carry the label SELF-REPORTED. From P01 on, each phase report points to that file. Every RED rating becomes an entry in the project risk register at the next risk review and is named in the STOP report that asks the owner. The measure changes no gate and blocks no phase by itself; the owner reads it and decides.

<<<P6-S21>>>
6.21 CORE VIABILITY REVIEW (after P08)
After the first nine phases the owner decides, on measured evidence, whether the core can carry the rest. It is a review and not a phase or a gate: it blocks nothing by itself, and the owner's next command is the decision to continue; a decision file in docs/decisions/ narrows, changes or stops the work. At P08-END1 the agent also writes `docs/phase-reports/P08-viability.md`. It is a separate file, not a field of the phase report, like the overflow file of Part 1 section 9.3. It is filled only from gate outputs and recorded files, each value with its command and exit code (11.8): (a) the evaluation of the Extract stage on the SYNTHETIC set against the thresholds of Part 4 section 4.5, per document type; (b) the model cost and the tokens per case from the model-call records and `docs/state/telemetry.json`, against the per-case budget of Part 3 section 3.14; (c) the walking-skeleton run with recorded responses: determinism and replay; (d) the coverage and mutation baselines against their floors; (e) the process-health table of 6.20 for P00 to P08; (f) the open verifications V-23 to V-26 and the data stage of 2.18; (g) every measure that is still NOT_MEASURED. The file ends with one line per question, answered YES, NO or NOT_MEASURED and never with a recommendation: all model thresholds of Part 4 section 4.5 met; cost per case inside the budget; replay reproduces every skeleton decision; any health measure rated RED; all unverified items listed. P09 entry reports the owner's answer when a decision file exists and otherwise treats the owner's command as the decision to continue; a missing answer is never a STOP.

<<<P6-S22>>>
6.22 MEASUREMENT BASELINE
A number set before any measurement (a budget, threshold, limit, rate or count marked design parameter or ASSUMPTION) is a plan, not a fact. Rules. (1) The P00 run is the first measurement: its attempts, tool calls and tokens in its phase report and in `docs/state/telemetry.json` are the baseline of class L; after a hard stop the owner may add measured lines such as `P00 measured_tokens: <n>` to `docs/decisions/run-parameters.md`, which the agent reads and never edits. (2) From each phase end on, the gate stores the measured figures next to the planned ones, and `HEALTH.md` (6.20) shows planned against measured. (3) A planned number is replaced only by an owner decision in docs/decisions/ that cites the measured value; the agent never changes a threshold or a budget, and a measured value worse than its plan is reported and never hidden (Part 1 section 5.6). (4) Quality thresholds follow the ratchet of Part 4: the first measured baseline becomes the floor and never decreases. (5) While a number is unmeasured, every sentence in the README, the reports and the guide that uses it says planned or design parameter and never presents it as an achieved result. Measured baseline from the P00 phase report (SELF-REPORTED): 13 tasks, 19 attempts, 1.46 attempts per task; tool calls and tokens were not recorded as measured numbers, so budget use and overhead share are NOT_MEASURED for P00.

<<<PHASE-01>>>
PHASE 01 — RISK MANAGEMENT SYSTEM

2. Purpose: build the single, complete risk-management system of decision D-33 as the top-level folder `risk-management/`, with its tools and its first seeded content. From this phase on every phase files a risk review.

Context Pack: `<<<P3-S1>>>` `<<<P3-S8>>>` `<<<P3-S9>>>` `<<<P3-S11>>>` `<<<P3-S15>>>` `<<<P3-S16>>>` `<<<P3-S23>>>` `<<<P3-S24>>>` `<<<P3-S30>>>` `<<<P2-S12>>>` `<<<P2-S14>>>` `<<<P4-S1>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<P6-S5>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>` `<<<D06-S3>>>` `<<<D32-S2.3>>>` `<<<D32-S2.5>>>` `<<<P7-RISK>>>` (Part 7 Risk Management section: scales, threshold, seeded risk lists, runbook list) `<<<P6-S20>>>` `<<<P6-S22>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P01-T01 Folder, policy and methodology
- Files: `risk-management/INDEX.md` (generated later), `risk-management/policy/RISK-POLICY.md`, `risk-management/methodology/METHOD.md`, `risk-management/methodology/SCALES.yaml`.
- Steps: write the policy (scope: product risk in use, project risk in build, hazards, threats, model risk, data and legal risk, continuity; roles: only the owner accepts risk; the agent proposes, never accepts; no compliance claims; residual-risk statement wording) and the method (identification sources, scoring, treatment order eliminate, reduce, detect, transfer, accept; control lifecycle PLANNED, IMPLEMENTED, VERIFIED; review triggers) from `<<<P7-RISK>>>`. `SCALES.yaml` holds the 1 to 5 likelihood and severity scales with anchors, the score rule (likelihood times severity, 1 to 25), the register threshold above which residual risk needs an owner acceptance, and the review cadence. These numbers are design parameters (ASSUMPTION) registered as D-35 when the owner approves this phase.
- Accept: `risk validate scales` exit 0 (T-RISK-0001); the policy contains no sentence that claims compliance, certification or conformity with any standard (T-RISK-0002, phrase check from the list in `<<<P7-RISK>>>`); the methodology names every register file created in this phase.

P01-T02 Schemas and validation core
- Files: `risk-management/schemas/` (risk, control, hazard, threat, acceptance, review, incident, drill), `risk-management/tools/` (loader, validator, link checker).
- Steps: JSON Schemas for every record kind; fields include id, title, area, description, cause, consequence, inherent and residual likelihood and severity, treatment, controls, owner area, accountable (always OWNER for acceptance), status, review date (month), source (spec marker or verification id), labels. Validator checks: schema validity; unique ids; every referenced id exists; every control has a lifecycle status and a target phase while PLANNED; every VERIFIED control links to at least one existing test id in the test index; every risk has an area and an accountable role; review dates are current.
- Accept: tests T-RISK-0003 to T-RISK-0016: each rule has a failing fixture and a passing fixture; a control overdue (target phase reached, still PLANNED) fails; a VERIFIED control with an unknown test id fails.

P01-T03 Product risk register (seed)
- Files: `risk-management/registers/product-risks.yaml`.
- Steps: seed the product risks demanded by `<<<P7-RISK>>>` and the invariants and requirements: wrong ALLOW, wrong or invented extraction, document-borne prompt injection, rule pack stale or expired, wrong classification, missed screening hit, cross-tenant leakage, ledger tampering or loss, agent runaway, cost overrun, model provider outage or deprecation, solver plan wrong, personal data in logs, SYNTHETIC presented as real, explanation misread as advice, key compromise. Each links controls by id; controls may be PLANNED with a target phase.
- Accept: `risk validate registers` exit 0; every invariant I1 to I14 and requirement PR-01 to PR-17 is referenced by at least one risk or control (T-RISK-0017 coverage test against the lists in Part 3 §3.1 and Part 2 §2.12).

P01-T04 Project risk register and P00 review
- Files: `risk-management/registers/project-risks.yaml`, `risk-management/reviews/P00.md`.
- Steps: seed the project risks of `<<<P7-RISK>>>` (dev-agent drift or hallucinated fact, gate tampering, token budget overrun, dependency compromise, external terms not matching license or data conditions (V-01), unverified source used, directive integrity, single-owner dependency, loss of repository); write the review record for P00 using the review schema.
- Accept: `risk validate reviews` exit 0; the P00 review lists the risks and controls P00 created (gate runner, protected paths, hygiene, secrets, license, deps) with their lifecycle status.

P01-T05 Hazard analysis, threat model, model risk, data and legal risk (seed)
- Files: `risk-management/registers/hazards.yaml`, `threats.yaml`, `ai-model-risks.yaml`, `data-legal-risks.yaml`.
- Steps: hazards as chains hazard → cause → hazardous situation → harm → control, for the harmful outcomes of the lifecycle (wrong release decision, missed hold, wrong cost, wrong deadline); threats from Part 3 §3.15 with trust boundaries and the model-specific list (document-borne injection, exfiltration through model output, tool-authority escalation); the reference list edition is recorded as UNVERIFIED until V-28; model risks from decision-d06 safeguards S1 to S7; data and legal risks from the verification register (V-01, V-04, V-05, V-07, V-08, V-16).
- Accept: every entry validates; every threat of Part 3 §3.15 has an entry (T-RISK-0018); every safeguard S1 to S7 of decision-d06 maps to a model risk and a control (T-RISK-0019); every V-item cited exists in the verification register list of `<<<P7-RISK>>>`.

P01-T06 Control catalogue
- Files: `risk-management/controls/catalogue.yaml`.
- Steps: one entry per control: id, type (preventive, detective, corrective), mechanism, implementing path or phase, verifying test ids (or target test ids while PLANNED), target phase. Seed the controls created so far and the planned controls referenced by the seeded risks.
- Accept: no orphan control (every control is used by a risk, hazard, threat or model risk); no risk without a control or an explicit acceptance request; `risk validate controls` exit 0 (T-RISK-0020, T-RISK-0021).

P01-T07 Acceptance, incident and continuity records
- Files: `risk-management/acceptance/_TEMPLATE.md`, `risk-management/incidents/LOG.yaml`, `risk-management/runbooks/_TEMPLATE.md`, `risk-management/continuity/DRILLS.yaml`, schemas validated.
- Steps: acceptance record format (risk id, residual score, reason, owner decision id in `docs/decisions/`, expiry, review date); empty valid incident log; runbook template (trigger, owner action, steps, verification, ledger record); drills register (restore, crypto-erasure, exit; planned dates month-level, results empty). Runbook contents are written by P12 and P28.
- Accept: `risk validate all` exit 0; an acceptance record without a matching decision file fails (T-RISK-0022); an expired acceptance fails (T-RISK-0023).

P01-T08 Report generator, review command and gate activation
- Files: `risk-management/tools/` (report, review, health), `risk-management/report/RESIDUAL.md` (generated), `risk-management/report/HEALTH.md` (generated), `risk-management/INDEX.md` (generated), edit `tools/gate/catalogue.toml` (activate `risk`), edit `tools/gate/protected.toml` (add the acceptance directory and the thresholds file), update `docs/state/protected-hashes.json` through `gate protect --update`.

Activate the `risk` gate in the catalogue.
- Accept: tests T-RISK-0024 to T-RISK-0036: `HEALTH.md` is byte-identical on regeneration; a measure with no data is rated NOT_MEASURED and never GREEN; a seeded RED rating becomes a risk-register entry in the review draft; the P00 baseline row shows 13 tasks and 19 attempts; regeneration is byte-identical; a handwritten edit of `RESIDUAL.md` fails the gate; the review command lists a seeded above-threshold risk and exits 1 until an acceptance exists; `gate run risk` exit 0 on the branch.

P01-RISK Risk review: run `risk review --phase 01`; update registers for what P01 created (the validator, the report generator and the `risk` gate become controls with status VERIFIED); write `reviews/P01.md`; list residual risks above the threshold as a STOP report asking the owner; never accept a risk (Part 6 §6.5).

P01-END1, P01-END2: per Part 1 §10.

8. Risk review: task P01-RISK above.

<<<PHASE-02>>>
PHASE 02 — SOURCE REGISTRY AND DATA GOVERNANCE

5. Context Pack: `<<<P3-S8>>>` `<<<P3-S12>>>` `<<<P2-S14>>>` `<<<P4-S12>>>` `<<<P4-S3>>>` `<<<P6-S5>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>` `<<<P8-SOURCES>>>` `<<<P8-DEPS>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P02-T01 Label taxonomy
- Files: `schemas/labels.json`, `data/labels/` (pure helper module).
- Steps: one machine-readable taxonomy: the seven labels, the strength order (OFFICIAL > DERIVED > SECONDARY > SIMULATED_RULE = SYNTHETIC > ASSUMPTION > UNVERIFIED), the weakest-of rule for derived data, the ALLOW-eligible set (OFFICIAL, DERIVED). The helper exposes order comparison and weakest-of; the kernel types of P03 are generated from this file.
- Accept: tests T-DATA-0001 to T-DATA-0006: order is a total preorder with the stated tie; weakest-of is associative, commutative and idempotent (property tests); only OFFICIAL and DERIVED are ALLOW-eligible; an unknown label is rejected.

P02-T02 Source Registry
- Files: `schemas/source.json`, `data/registry/sources.yaml`, `data/registry/BASELINE.json` (protected).
- Steps: entries carry source_id, publisher, URL or document reference, retrieval month (month-level), label, license summary, redistributable flag, kind (SOURCE, DATASET, DEPENDENCY, STANDARD), status; a validator enforces uniqueness, month-level dates, label presence and that a redistributable flag exists. `BASELINE.json` records the content hash of each entry; it changes only through `gate data --update-baseline` in a task that names the path.
- Accept: tests T-DATA-0007 to T-DATA-0014: missing field fails; duplicate id fails; day-level date fails; an entry changed without a baseline update fails; a baseline update outside a named task is refused.

P02-T03 License Manifest and Source Gap Register
- Files: `schemas/license-manifest.json`, `schemas/gap.json`, `data/manifests/`, `data/registry/gaps.yaml`.
- Steps: a manifest per dataset (terms, attribution text, permitted uses, redistribution permission); the gap register lists needed but unverified facts with the next check and the verification id (V-xx). A dataset without a manifest, or with an UNVERIFIED license, is excluded: the data gate fails if any file under `data/` is not covered by a manifest, or if any non-excluded file's license is UNVERIFIED.
- Accept: tests T-DATA-0015 to T-DATA-0022: uncovered file fails; UNVERIFIED license on a used dataset fails; third-party table detection fixture fails (no stored copyrighted tables, Part 3 §3.12); gap entry without verification id fails.

P02-T04 Seed content
- Files: entries in `data/registry/sources.yaml`, `data/manifests/`, `data/registry/gaps.yaml`.
- Steps: transcribe exactly the entries of `<<<P8-SOURCES>>>` with the labels and license summaries given there; every entry not given a verified status is UNVERIFIED and listed in the gap register with its V-item; add a registry entry of kind DEPENDENCY for each package in `docs/dependencies.json`. Nothing is added from memory (Part 1 §6.2); a needed source that is absent is a STOP.
- Accept: the number of transcribed entries equals the number in `<<<P8-SOURCES>>>` (T-DATA-0023); every V-item of the verification register that concerns data (V-04 to V-09, V-16) has a gap entry or a recorded verified source (T-DATA-0024).

P02-T05 Generated dependency view and gates
