P02-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: add data and legal risks closed or reduced by the new gates (V-04, V-05, V-07, V-08, V-16 stay open until verified), controls for registry and manifest integrity, review record `reviews/P02.md`.

P02-END1, P02-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gates: `data`, `labels` (P). Tier A runs in full. Test ids T-DATA-0001 to T-DATA-0030.

8. Risk review: task P02-RISK.

9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: `gate run data` and `gate run labels` exit 0; the registry baseline recorded; every verification item that concerns data has a gap or a source; `docs/dependencies.json` regenerates byte-identically; README Build Progress ticked by the gate.

10. Look-ahead.
- P03 generates kernel label and status types from `schemas/labels.json`; do not change its field names.
- P05 and later add search-index configuration hashes and pack source references that cite registry ids; keep source ids stable.

11. Budget: class M; retries 3; tool calls 400; token budget named in the owner's command.
<<<END-PHASE-02>>>

<<<PHASE-03>>>
PHASE 03 — KERNEL CONTRACTS

1. Header: P03 · Kernel contracts · branch `phase/03-kernel-contracts` · class L · capabilities: none.

2. Purpose: define, once and as generated code, the typed contracts every later phase builds on: entities, canonical serialization, hashing, labels, the decision lattice, the case state machine, errors, configuration and ports. Contracts are registered by hash from this phase on and change only through change tasks (Part 6 §6.12).

3. Entry conditions beyond Part 1 §8: `schemas/labels.json` exists and validates; `gate list` shows `arch`, `generated-files`, `contract` and `property` as NOT_ACTIVE with `activates_in` P03; `risk validate all` exit 0. Evidence: the commands.

4. Scope fence. May create: `schemas/`, `kernel/` (contracts, canonical, hashing, labels, decision, casestate, errors, config, ports), `tools/schemagen/`, `contracts.lock`, `test-house/kernel/`, `docs/ledger/P03.md`, risk-management records. May edit `tools/gate/catalogue.toml` (activation entries), `pyproject.toml` (import-linter and lint configuration only). Commands: P00 set, the schema generator, import-linter through the gate. Dependencies only from the P03 set of `<<<P8-DEPS>>>`.
Scope paths: schemas/**, kernel/**, tools/schemagen/**, tools/gate/gates/**, tools/gate/catalogue.toml, tools/gate/protected.toml, contracts.lock, pyproject.toml, test-house/kernel/**, docs/ledger/P03.md, docs/phase-reports/**, docs/state/**, docs/decisions/**, risk-management/**, CHANGELOG.md, README.md

5. Context Pack: `<<<P3-S1>>>` `<<<P3-S2>>>` `<<<P3-S3>>>` `<<<P3-S4>>>` `<<<P3-S5>>>` `<<<P3-S8>>>` `<<<P3-S14>>>` `<<<P3-S17>>>` `<<<P3-S18>>>` `<<<P3-S21>>>` `<<<P3-S22>>>` `<<<P3-S24>>>` `<<<P3-S31>>>` `<<<P4-S4>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<P6-S10>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>` `<<<P2-S11>>>` (case-outcome composition rule only).

6. Tasks.

P03-T01 Schemas and generator
- Files: `schemas/` (common types and the fifteen entities of Part 3 §3.3), `tools/schemagen/`, `kernel/contracts/_generated/`.
- Steps: JSON Schema files for Tenant, Case, Document, Fact, RuleResult, Proposal, Decision, EvidenceRecord, Approval, Passport, Pack, Source, MemoryEntry, Simulation, AutonomyPolicy, each with tenant_id and schema_version; the generator emits frozen, strictly typed models and validators, marks every generated file, and is reproducible. Activate `generated-files`.
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0001 to T-KRN-0008: regeneration is byte-identical; a hand edit of a generated file fails the gate; every entity has tenant_id and schema_version; models are immutable (mutation returns a new value); no untyped dictionary crosses a module boundary.

P03-T02 Canonical serialization and domain-separated hashing
- Files: `kernel/canonical/`, `kernel/hashing/`, `schemas/hash-tags.json`.
- Steps: canonical JSON per RFC 8785 for every hashed or signed byte sequence (implementation or library as fixed in `<<<P8-DEPS>>>`, V-20); SHA-256 with the domain tags of Part 3 §3.5 registered in one tag registry (each tag unique per purpose and versioned); every stored hash carries an algorithm identifier.
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0009 to T-KRN-0018: known-answer vectors from RFC 8785 and SHA-256; a value hashed under one tag never equals the same value under another tag (cross-purpose test); decimals serialize without exponent or trailing zeros ambiguity; key order and number formatting are stable under random input order (property test); unknown tag rejected.

P03-T03 Core value types and the decision lattice
- Files: `kernel/types/` (identifiers, money, time, quantity, label, status, decision), `kernel/ports/`.
- Steps: opaque, time-sortable identifiers from an injected source; Money as fixed-point decimal with ISO 4217 code and explicit rounding mode, no floating point; instants in UTC and whole-second durations; quantities with explicit unit; Label with order and weakest-of generated from `schemas/labels.json`; DecisionValue with the lattice ALLOW < ABSTAIN < HOLD < ESCALATE and `combine` taking the most severe while keeping all reasons; ports for Clock, IdSource and RandomSource. The kernel logic that decides stays synchronous and pure; the ports that reach persistence are asynchronous interfaces (P04), so the database driver can change inside `adapters/postgres/` only.
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0019 to T-KRN-0030: lattice combine is associative, commutative, idempotent and monotone (property tests); the combined reasons equal the union of inputs; floats are rejected in Money and Quantity; rounding mode is mandatory; weakest-of agrees with the P02 helper on all label pairs.

P03-T04 Facts, statuses and ALLOW eligibility
- Files: `kernel/facts/`, `kernel/decision/`.
- Steps: pure functions for fact status transitions (PROPOSED, VERIFIED, CONFLICTED, SUPERSEDED, REJECTED), conflict detection per fact key (two or more non-superseded values), supersession by reference, and the ALLOW eligibility function of Part 3 §3.8 and decision D-26: ALLOW requires every required fact present and VERIFIED, no rule failure and every contributing datum OFFICIAL or DERIVED; a SECONDARY-or-weaker datum yields ABSTAIN with a reason naming it; on a case whose `data_origin` is not SYNTHETIC, an ALLOW whose `calibration_basis` (Part 5 §5.1.6a) holds an artifact of class CALIBRATED_SYNTHETIC is lowered to ESCALATE with the reason `<ID>.REVIEW.UNVALIDATED_CALIBRATION`, and a capability that returns ALLOW without declaring its basis is rejected by the contract; `data_origin` SYNTHETIC may reach ALLOW but is stamped SYNTHETIC. Rules read only VERIFIED facts: expose a reader that cannot return another status.
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0031 to T-KRN-0042: every status transition allowed or rejected as tabulated; a conflicted fact key yields ESCALATE with both citations; a missing fact yields ABSTAIN; SECONDARY input never gives ALLOW; SYNTHETIC-origin ALLOW carries the stamp; a real-data ALLOW with a CALIBRATED_SYNTHETIC artifact becomes ESCALATE and with a VALIDATED_TENANT artifact stays ALLOW; an ALLOW without a declared basis is rejected; the reader never returns a non-VERIFIED fact.

P03-T05 Case state machine
- Files: `kernel/casestate/`, `schemas/case-states.json`.
- Steps: the states and side states of Part 3 §3.4 as a transition table; guards; the idempotency-key contract type (same key returns the original result); the version-check contract (stale version rejected with a conflict); timeouts move to BLOCKED and never approve; re-evaluation supersedes by reference.
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0043 to T-KRN-0052: a generated test covers every (state, event) pair and asserts the table outcome; undefined transitions are rejected; the same idempotency key returns an identical result object; a stale version yields the conflict error; two approvals of one proposal: the first wins.

P03-T06 Error model
- Files: `schemas/errors.json`, `kernel/errors/`.
- Steps: one registry of stable error codes mapped to the RFC 9457 problem-details structure; typed result types for expected failures; exceptions reserved for defects.
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0053 to T-KRN-0058: every code has title, status and documentation text; codes are unique and never reused (compared with the frozen registry); the problem-details object validates; a broad exception handler in the core is rejected by lint.

P03-T07 Configuration contract
- Files: `schemas/config.json`, `kernel/config/`.
- Steps: typed configuration with the defaults of Part 3 §3.14 (20 model calls, 60,000 tokens, 120 s, 50 tool calls, 200 steps per case), validated at start, canonical hash function for the configuration hash that later phases record on the deployment chain and in every Decision.
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0059 to T-KRN-0064: invalid configuration is rejected; the hash is stable and changes with any field; defaults equal the registry parameters; unknown keys are rejected.

P03-T08 Architecture enforcement and contract registry
- Files: import-linter contracts in `pyproject.toml`, lint rules for the core, `tools/gate/gates/contract.py`, `contracts.lock` (protected), edit `tools/gate/catalogue.toml` (activate `arch`, `contract`, `property`), `tools/gate/protected.toml`.
- Steps: encode the import rules of Part 3 §3.18; fail the `arch` gate when any of the six display names of Part 3 §3.9 occurs in `kernel/`, `schemas/`, `capabilities/`, `agents/`, `policies/` or a fixture identifier (they are allowed only in the text catalogue of the views and in documentation); declare the closed role-id list in the schemas; ban in the core floating-point money, wall-clock, randomness, UUID and environment access (Part 4 §4.4); record in `contracts.lock` the content hash of the shared contracts (all of `schemas/`, the hash-tag registry, the error registry, the case-state table); the gate fails when a hash changes without a change task `Pnn-CHG` in the same phase (Part 6 §6.12).
- Accept: tests T-KRN-0065 to T-KRN-0074: a seeded forbidden import fails; a seeded display name in a schema or a fixture identifier fails; an actor outside the closed kinds and role ids is rejected; a seeded clock call in the core fails; a modified schema without a change task fails and with one passes; `contracts.lock` regenerates identically; `gate run arch contract property generated-files` exit 0.

P03-T09 Mutation baseline for the kernel
- Files: baselines through the gate only.
- Steps: run the mutation gate on `kernel/` and record the first floor; coverage floors for `kernel/` set to the measured values, which must meet the thresholds of Part 4 §4.5 (branch coverage 95 percent, mutation score 85 percent for the kernel). Run the mutation gate also on `tools/gate/` and record its floor, which must meet the gate-code threshold of Part 4 §4.5; a surviving mutant in a gate is killed by a new fixture or recorded as equivalent with a reason. Activate `mutation` for changed modules (including `tools/gate/`).
- Accept: `gate run mutation` exit 0; the baselines meet the thresholds; below-threshold results are a defect to fix with tests, never a threshold change.

P03-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for lattice monotonicity, label ordering, state machine completeness, canonical hashing and cross-purpose hash separation; update the registers; `reviews/P03.md`.

P03-END1, P03-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gates: `arch`, `generated-files`, `contract`, `property`, `mutation` (changed modules). Tier A: fast kernel tests; Tier B: property and mutation selected by impact (kernel schema change selects all). Test ids T-KRN-0001 to T-KRN-0074.

8. Risk review: task P03-RISK.

9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: all kernel gates exit 0; `contracts.lock` consistent; kernel coverage and mutation baselines at or above the thresholds; README Build Progress ticked by the gate; risk review filed.

10. Look-ahead.
- P04 builds persistence on these contracts: every table carries tenant_id and its forced policy; repositories implement the ports of this phase; do not add storage code here.
- P05 builds the Evidence Ledger on the canonical, hashing and tag registry of T02; the tag registry already holds the tags of Part 3 §3.5.

11. Budget: class L; retries 3; tool calls 700; token budget named in the owner's command.
<<<END-PHASE-03>>>

<<<PHASE-04>>>
PHASE 04 — PERSISTENCE AND TENANCY BASELINE

1. Header: P04 · Persistence and tenancy baseline · branch `phase/04-persistence-tenancy` · class L · capabilities: none.

2. Purpose: give every later phase one storage layer: PostgreSQL schema and migrations generated from the contracts, repositories in two implementations, an atomic write path with outbox and idempotency, the append-only database guard and the row-level security baseline. Isolation is proven from the first table, before any ledger or capability exists.

3. Entry conditions beyond Part 1 §8: `contracts.lock` consistent; `gate list` shows `integration` and `migration` as NOT_ACTIVE with `activates_in` P04; the kernel ports of P03 (repository, clock, id, transaction) exist; an ephemeral PostgreSQL and the policy engine start from the lock file. Evidence: the commands.

4. Scope fence. May create: `kernel/persistence/` (ports binding, in-memory repositories, transaction scope, outbox, idempotency store), `adapters/postgres/` (repositories, migrations, role definitions, tenant context binding), `tools/migrate/`, `tools/threat-matrix/`, `tools/faultlab/` (crash-point injection), `test-house/persistence/`, `test-house/tenancy/`, `docs/ledger/P04.md`, risk-management records. May edit `tools/gate/catalogue.toml` (activation entries), `tools/gate/gates/`, `contracts.lock` only through a change task. Everything else is forbidden. Commands: P00 set, the migration tool, the ephemeral database start script.
Scope paths: kernel/persistence/**, adapters/postgres/**, tools/migrate/**, tools/threat-matrix/**, tools/faultlab/**, tools/gate/gates/**, tools/gate/catalogue.toml, tools/gate/protected.toml, contracts.lock, test-house/persistence/**, test-house/tenancy/**, docs/ledger/P04.md, docs/phase-reports/**, docs/state/**, docs/decisions/**, risk-management/**, CHANGELOG.md, README.md, tools/schemagen/**

5. Context Pack: `<<<P3-S3>>>` `<<<P3-S4>>>` `<<<P3-S16>>>` `<<<P3-S18>>>` `<<<P3-S20>>>` `<<<P3-S24>>>` `<<<P3-S27>>>` `<<<P3-S31>>>` `<<<P4-S4>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<D32-S2.1>>>` `<<<P6-S10>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P04-T01 Ephemeral database, roles and migration tool
- Files: `tools/migrate/`, `adapters/postgres/roles/`, `adapters/postgres/migrations/0001_*`.
- Steps: a migration tool that applies forward migrations under a separate migration role; three roles: migration owner, application (no superuser, no bypass, owns no table, cannot alter policies), read-only audit. An ephemeral database start script for tests. Activate `integration`.
- Accept: tests T-DB-0001 to T-DB-0008: roles have exactly the stated privileges; the application role cannot create, alter or drop; the ephemeral database starts from the lock file only; `gate run integration` exit 0.

P04-T02 Schema from the contracts
- Files: `adapters/postgres/migrations/`, generator extension in `tools/schemagen/` for table definitions.
- Steps: one table per persisted entity of Part 3 §3.3, generated from `schemas/`; every table carries `tenant_id`, `schema_version` and a time-sortable primary key; tenant-scoped uniqueness (no global counters, no shared sequences that reveal other tenants); expand-then-contract convention recorded in the migration header.
- Accept: tests T-DB-0009 to T-DB-0018: every persisted entity has a table; a table without tenant_id fails the migration lint; the generated schema regenerates byte-identical; a foreign-tenant row cannot satisfy a foreign key.

P04-T03 Row-level security baseline
- Files: `adapters/postgres/policies/`, `adapters/postgres/tenant_context.py`, migration lint in `tools/migrate/lint/`.
- Steps: row-level security enabled and forced on every table with a deny-by-default policy keyed on a transaction-local tenant setting; the setting is applied at transaction start and cleared on release; the tenant context object cannot be built without a principal (Part 3 §3.24); migration lint fails any new table without a forced policy and any security-definer function or view not listed in a policy review test.
- Accept: tests T-DB-0019 to T-DB-0034, run as the application role: no read or write without a tenant setting; a foreign tenant sees zero rows; a forced policy applies to the table owner role; the pooled-connection test shows the setting cannot leak between two connections reusing one session; the lint rejects a seeded table without a policy.

P04-T04 Repositories in two implementations
- Files: `kernel/persistence/repositories/`, `adapters/postgres/repositories/`.
- Steps: repository interfaces from the P03 ports, asynchronous (coroutines); an in-memory implementation for unit tests that implements the same asynchronous interface, and a PostgreSQL implementation through the asynchronous driver named in `<<<P8-DEPS>>>`; both take the tenant context; there is no unscoped query in application code; parameterized queries only; optimistic concurrency through the row version (Part 3 §3.4).
- Accept: tests T-DB-0035 to T-DB-0048: one contract test suite runs against both implementations with equal results; a stale version write is rejected with a conflict error; a query without a tenant context does not compile (type check) and fails at run time if forced.

P04-T05 Atomic write path, outbox and idempotency
- Files: `kernel/persistence/unit_of_work.py`, `outbox.py`, `idempotency.py`, `adapters/postgres/outbox/`.
- Steps: a unit of work that commits a state change, its event and its outbox row in one transaction or none; an outbox relay that delivers each event exactly once to its consumer by lease and acknowledgement; an idempotency store keyed by tenant and idempotency key returning the original result for a repeated command; poison events go to a dead-letter table with an alert record (Part 3 §3.27).
- Accept: tests T-DB-0049 to T-DB-0060: a failure after the state change and before the outbox row leaves no state change; a repeated command with the same key changes nothing and returns the first result; two concurrent identical commands produce one effect; a dead-lettered event is visible and not lost.

P04-T06 Append-only database guard
- Files: `adapters/postgres/migrations/` (guard), `tools/migrate/lint/append_only.py`, `tools/gate/gates/migration.py`.
- Steps: UPDATE and DELETE privileges are not granted to the application role on ledger and evidence tables and a guard trigger rejects them; the migration lint fails any migration that weakens the guard; the `migration` gate applies all migrations forward on a database seeded with realistic synthetic rows and runs the policy lint. Activate `migration`.
- Accept: tests T-DB-0061 to T-DB-0068: an update and a delete by the application role fail; a seeded migration that grants update fails the lint; forward migration on seeded data passes; `gate run migration` exit 0.

P04-T07 Crash-consistency laboratory (raise level)
- Files: `tools/faultlab/`, `test-house/persistence/crash/`.
- Steps: a fault-injection harness with named kill points inside the unit of work (before commit, after commit before outbox delivery, during delivery, during a migration); each scenario restarts the components and compares the recovered state with the expected state by hash. The lab is a shared tool: later phases register their own kill points.
- Accept: tests T-DB-0069 to T-DB-0078: every kill point recovers to a state equal to the expected hash; no partial write is visible; the lab reports the list of registered kill points and fails if a registered point has no scenario.

P04-T08 Threat-to-test matrix tool (raise level)
- Files: `tools/threat-matrix/`, `docs/state/threat-catalog.json` (generated from the directive catalogs), `test-house/tenancy/`.
- Steps: a tool that reads the attack catalog of Part 3 §3.24 and the threat list of Part 3 §3.15, assigns every item an identifier, and reads the threat ids named in test headers; it prints items without a test. This phase maps the database-level and repository-level items; later phases map the rest. Enforcement by the `trace` gate starts in P09; until then the report lists the coverage.
- Accept: tests T-DB-0079 to T-DB-0084: the catalog parses to the full item list; a seeded item without a test is listed; the mapping of this phase covers the identifier-reference, row-level security bypass, pooled-connection and shared-sequence items.

P04-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for cross-tenant read and write, session setting leakage, partial writes, duplicate effects, weakened append-only guard; update the registers; `reviews/P04.md`.

P04-END1, P04-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gates: `integration`, `migration`. Tier A: in-memory repository and tenant context tests. Tier B: all PostgreSQL tests (a change to migrations, policies, schemas or the generator selects all). Test ids T-DB-0001 to T-DB-0084.

8. Risk review: task P04-RISK.

9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: `integration` and `migration` exit 0; the isolation tests pass as the application role; the crash laboratory has no unmatched kill point; `contracts.lock` consistent; threat-matrix coverage report attached; README Build Progress ticked by the gate; risk review filed.

10. Look-ahead.
- P05 writes ledger chains on the append-only guard and the unit of work; the ledger tables are created there under the policies of this phase, using the same lint.
- P07 uses the outbox and idempotency store for the orchestrator; P10 exposes idempotency keys through the API.

11. Budget: class L; retries 3; tool calls 700; token budget named in the owner's command.
<<<END-PHASE-04>>>

<<<PHASE-05>>>
PHASE 05 — EVIDENCE LEDGER

1. Header: P05 · Evidence Ledger · branch `phase/05-evidence-ledger` · class L · capabilities: none.

2. Purpose: build the tamper-evident record of everything the system does: case and system chains, Merkle tree, commitments, signed checkpoints, proofs, verification, replay, a self-contained case bundle and an independent verifier written separately from the kernel.

3. Entry conditions beyond Part 1 §8: P04 gates exit 0; the hash tag registry holds the tags of Part 3 §3.5; the unit of work and the append-only guard exist. Evidence: `gate run integration migration`.

4. Scope fence. May create: `kernel/ledger/` (records, chains, tree, commitments, checkpoints, proofs, verify, replay, bundle), `adapters/postgres/ledger/` (tables, repositories, migrations under the P04 lint), `adapters/signing/` (Ed25519 signer port and a development implementation), `tools/witness/` (witness component and anchor port with the offline-export adapter), `tools/verifier/` (independent verifier), `tools/gate/gates/replay.py`, `test-house/ledger/`, `test-house/vectors/`, `docs/ledger/P05.md`, risk-management records. The signing key used in tests is generated per run; no key material is committed. Everything else is forbidden.
Scope paths: kernel/ledger/**, adapters/postgres/ledger/**, adapters/postgres/migrations/**, adapters/signing/**, tools/verifier/**, tools/witness/**, tools/faultlab/**, tools/threat-matrix/**, tools/gate/gates/**, tools/gate/catalogue.toml, contracts.lock, test-house/ledger/**, test-house/vectors/**, docs/ledger/P05.md, docs/phase-reports/**, docs/state/**, docs/decisions/**, risk-management/**, CHANGELOG.md, README.md, docs/ledger-format.md

5. Context Pack: `<<<P3-S2>>>` `<<<P3-S5>>>` `<<<P3-S10>>>` `<<<P3-S21>>>` `<<<P3-S22>>>` `<<<P3-S24>>>` `<<<P3-S29>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<P4-S14>>>` `<<<D32-S2.3>>>` `<<<P6-S10>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P05-T01 Case chain records
- Files: `kernel/ledger/records.py`, `chain.py`, ledger tables and migrations.
- Steps: the record of Part 3 §3.5 with gap-free sequence per chain under a per-case lock, `record_hash` with tag `ctc/record/v1`, algorithm identifier on every stored hash, payloads stored by reference, with the salted payload hash and the salt_id of 3.5; ledger tables created with forced policy and the append-only guard through the P04 migration lint.
- Accept: tests T-LED-0001 to T-LED-0012: sequence has no gaps under concurrent appends; every field change alters the record hash; an update or delete is rejected by the database; a record of one tenant is invisible to another; with the salt destroyed a low-entropy payload cannot be confirmed by guessing.

P05-T02 System chain
- Files: `kernel/ledger/system_chain.py`.
- Steps: the deployment chain (configuration hash at start, deployment key rotation, deployment identity, deployment kill switch, deployment-level pack and policy activation) and one tenant system chain per tenant (pack activation set and overlays, memory and autonomy policy, tenant kill switch); every case record carries the tenant system chain position in force and the signed deployment attestation of 3.5, never the deployment chain position.
- Accept: tests T-LED-0013 to T-LED-0020: each event type appends once; a case record without a tenant system position or deployment attestation is rejected; each system chain verifies independently of any case chain; the tree size, sequence numbers and proofs visible to one tenant are identical whatever another tenant does.

P05-T03 Merkle tree, checkpoints and proofs
- Files: `kernel/ledger/tree.py`, `checkpoint.py`, `proofs.py`, `adapters/signing/`.
- Steps: the RFC 9162 style tree with tags `ctc/leaf/v1` and `ctc/node/v1` and its split rule; Ed25519 signed checkpoints every 100 records and at case close; inclusion and consistency proofs; the signer is a port, so the key lives outside the kernel; the witness component of 3.5 co-signs a checkpoint only as a consistent extension of its last co-signed checkpoint, and the anchor port has the offline-export adapter.
- Accept: tests T-LED-0021 to T-LED-0036: known-answer vectors for leaf, node, root and proofs; property tests over random trees for both proof kinds; a changed byte in a record, checkpoint or signature is detected; a proof for one purpose fails when replayed for another; the witness refuses a checkpoint that rewrites history; a checkpoint without a valid co-signature is reported SELF-ATTESTED.

P05-T04 Commitments
- Files: `kernel/ledger/commitments.py`.
- Steps: the evaluation commitment (facts set, pack set, evaluation time, configuration hash), the proposal commitment, and salted field commitments with tag `ctc/commit/v1`; salts are referenced from the encrypted payload store port; destroying a salt makes the commitment unlinkable.
- Accept: tests T-LED-0037 to T-LED-0046: equal inputs give equal commitments; any input change gives a different value; a revealed value and salt verifies; with the salt destroyed the value cannot be confirmed; cross-purpose reuse fails.

P05-T05 Verify and replay
- Files: `kernel/ledger/verify.py`, `replay.py`, `tools/gate/gates/replay.py`.
- Steps: an offline verify with the modes `--structural` (no payload needed, works after crypto-erasure) and `--full` (payload hashes and commitments recomputed; erased records reported ERASED, not as a failure) that reports the first broken record; replay of a case export re-runs the deterministic stages from recorded inputs and recorded model outputs and compares every result; the replay port needs no network; the verify result is exactly one of SELF-ATTESTED, WITNESSED-LOCAL, WITNESSED-EXTERNAL and ANCHORED (3.5). Activate `replay`.
- Accept: tests T-LED-0047 to T-LED-0058: a clean ledger verifies at the level its evidence supports, and each of the four levels is reproduced by a fixture; the bare word verified is never printed; a seeded tamper in each field is reported with the first broken record; after a test erasure `--structural` passes and `--full` reports ERASED; replay equality on a stub case; replay with network access blocked passes.

P05-T06 Case bundle (raise level)
- Files: `kernel/ledger/bundle.py`, `docs/ledger-format.md` (generated from the schemas).
- Steps: a self-contained export bundle: manifest with hashes, case chain, tenant system chain segment, the deployment attestations, checkpoints, proofs, payload references with their hashes, pack versions and public key history, witness co-signatures with the witness key history and anchor receipts; deterministic archive layout; the bundle verifies offline with no database, no network and no running service.
- Accept: tests T-LED-0059 to T-LED-0066: export and import round-trip gives equal hashes; a bundle with one altered file fails verification; the bundle layout is byte-identical across two exports of the same case.

P05-T07 Independent verifier (raise level)
- Files: `tools/verifier/` (standard library only, no import from `kernel/`), `test-house/vectors/`.
- Steps: a second implementation of record, tree, checkpoint, proof, commitment and bundle verification written from the format document and the vectors, not from the kernel code; a differential test requires equal verdicts and equal verification levels on valid and tampered bundles; the verifier is built into a single-file tool that is shipped separately in release (D-32 section 2.3).
- Accept: tests T-LED-0067 to T-LED-0078: equal verdicts with the kernel verifier on at least 50 generated bundles including seeded tampers; the verifier imports no project module (checked by the architecture test); `gate run replay` exit 0 including the differential.

P05-T08 Crash points and threat mapping
- Files: `tools/faultlab/` registrations, `tools/threat-matrix/` mappings.
- Steps: register kill points inside append, checkpoint write and export; map the ledger and proof endpoints items and the evidence tampering items of the threat catalog to tests of this phase.
- Accept: tests T-LED-0079 to T-LED-0086: recovery after a kill at each point leaves a verifiable ledger with no gap; mapped items have tests.

P05-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for tampering, gaps, replay of hashes across purposes, signature misuse, verifier divergence, salt loss; update the registers; `reviews/P05.md`.

P05-END1, P05-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gate: `replay`. Tier A: vectors and unit tests. Tier B: PostgreSQL ledger, differential verifier, crash points. Test ids T-LED-0001 to T-LED-0086.

8. Risk review: task P05-RISK.

9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: `replay` and all earlier gates exit 0; the independent verifier agrees with the kernel verifier on the generated set; the ledger format document is generated and consistent; `contracts.lock` includes the ledger formats; risk review filed; README ticked by the gate.

10. Look-ahead.
- P06 evaluates rules and records RuleResults and the evaluation commitment through the record types of this phase.
- P09 builds the walking skeleton whose Passport is replayed and verified with the bundle and the independent verifier.

11. Budget: class L; retries 3; tool calls 700; token budget named in the owner's command.

<<<PHASE-06>>>
PHASE 06 — RULE ENGINE

1. Header: P06 · Rule Engine · branch `phase/06-rule-engine` · class L · capabilities: none.

2. Purpose: build the deterministic core that turns VERIFIED Facts and versioned Rule Packs into RuleResults: Pack DSL, reference interpreter, generated evaluator, validity windows, conflict handling and an environment-perturbation determinism gate.

3. Entry conditions beyond Part 1 §8: P05 gates exit 0; the decision lattice, labels and fact status exist in the kernel; `gate list` shows `differential`, `metamorphic` and `perf` as NOT_ACTIVE with `activates_in` P06. Evidence: the commands.

4. Scope fence. May create: `kernel/rules/` (DSL types, parser, validator, interpreter, evaluator interface, conflict resolution, pack loader), `tools/rulegen/` (evaluator generator), `tools/packkit/` (the Pack Authoring Kit), `packs/_template/` (pack skeleton), `packs/_fixtures/` (test packs, SYNTHETIC), `schemas/pack.json` additions through a change task, `test-house/rules/`, `docs/ledger/P06.md`, risk-management records. No real rule content of any capability is written here; the Pack Authoring Kit of P06-T08 is how real packs are written later. Everything else is forbidden.
Scope paths: kernel/rules/**, tools/rulegen/**, tools/packkit/**, packs/_template/**, packs/_fixtures/**, test-house/packkit/**, schemas/**, contracts.lock, tools/gate/gates/**, tools/gate/catalogue.toml, test-house/rules/**, docs/ledger/P06.md, docs/phase-reports/**, docs/state/**, docs/decisions/**, risk-management/**, CHANGELOG.md, README.md

5. Context Pack: `<<<P3-S3>>>` `<<<P3-S6>>>` `<<<P3-S8>>>` `<<<P3-S21>>>` `<<<P3-S22>>>` `<<<P3-S28>>>` `<<<P3-S31>>>` `<<<P4-S4>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<P4-S14>>>` `<<<P6-S10>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P06-T01 Pack DSL types and parser
- Files: `kernel/rules/dsl/`, `schemas/pack.json` (change task P06-CHG1 if the P03 schema differs).
- Steps: manifest and rule definitions of Part 3 §3.6; the restricted typed expression language: boolean operators, comparisons, decimal arithmetic, set membership, normalized string equality, calendar-pack date arithmetic, field access and quantifiers over finite collections; no loops, recursion, I/O, randomness, clock or user-defined functions.
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0001 to T-RUL-0014: every allowed construct parses; every forbidden construct is rejected with a stable error code; every expression terminates (checked by structural bound).

P06-CHG1 Change task, only if the pack schema of P03 must change: reason, consumers, lock update, consumer tests re-run, owner approval requested through a STOP report (Part 6 §6.12).

P06-T02 Pack validation and loading
- Files: `kernel/rules/validator.py`, `loader.py`.
- Steps: validate schema, window consistency, citation and label presence, a declared pack label no stronger than the weakest label among its registered sources (Part 3 §3.8), expression typing and precedence declarations; an invalid pack fails closed and a system-chain event is requested through the P05 interface; semantic versioning check: an outcome change requires a new version.
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0015 to T-RUL-0026: each invalidity class is rejected; overlapping windows without precedence are rejected; a rule without citation or label is rejected; a pack whose declared label is stronger than the weakest label among its registered sources is rejected; a valid pack loads and its hash is stable.

P06-T03 Reference interpreter
- Files: `kernel/rules/interpreter.py`.
- Steps: evaluate rules whose validity window contains the explicit evaluation time over VERIFIED facts only; record every rule considered with state APPLIED, NOT_APPLICABLE or SKIPPED (with reason); a missing fact gives ABSTAIN, a CONFLICTED fact gives ESCALATE with both citations; decimal arithmetic with an explicit context; no clock, no network.
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0027 to T-RUL-0042: window boundaries inclusive or exclusive as declared; absence of a finding is recorded as evidence; no rule reads a non-VERIFIED fact; money never uses floating point.

P06-T04 Evaluator generator
- Files: `tools/rulegen/`, `kernel/rules/_generated/`.
- Steps: generate typed evaluators from definitions; generated files are marked, reproducible and never edited; production uses the reference interpreter, the generated evaluator is the independent oracle of the differential test, replay may use either; a pack version registered after build time is activated only after the interpreter passes the fixtures and golden tests shipped in the pack, recorded on the deployment chain (the tenant system chain for an overlay). Activate `differential`.
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0043 to T-RUL-0052: byte-identical regeneration; the differential test gives identical results from both evaluators on all fixtures and on at least 1,000 generated random fact sets; a seeded divergence fails the gate.

P06-T05 Conflict resolution and combination
- Files: `kernel/rules/conflicts.py`, `combine.py`.
- Steps: declared precedence; unresolved conflicts yield ESCALATE with both citations; combining results takes the most severe and keeps all reasons (Part 3 §3.8); the evaluation commitment is produced through the P05 interface.
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0053 to T-RUL-0062: combination is monotone and order independent (property tests); the commitment reproduces on replay; ALLOW is impossible with a SECONDARY-or-weaker datum.

P06-T06 Metamorphic and performance gates
- Files: `test-house/rules/metamorphic/`, `test-house/rules/perf/`, gate entries.
- Steps: metamorphic relations (reordering facts, renaming ids, adding a non-referenced fact, shifting time within a window leave results unchanged); performance budget test for a case with 200 facts, p95 at most 200 ms (Part 4 §4.14), baseline recorded by the gate. Activate `metamorphic` and `perf` (budgeted paths).
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0063 to T-RUL-0072: all relations hold; the budget holds on the reference environment; a seeded slowdown above 10 percent fails.

P06-T07 Environment-perturbation determinism gate (raise level)
- Files: `test-house/rules/determinism/`, `tools/gate/gates/determinism.py`.
- Steps: run the same case set in separate processes under different hash seeds, locales, time zones, thread counts, working directories and dictionary insertion orders; compare result hashes bitwise; the gate is part of the `differential` set and of Tier B.
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0073 to T-RUL-0080: all perturbations give identical hashes; a seeded dependence on locale, time zone or iteration order is caught.

P06-T08 Pack Authoring Kit
- Files: `tools/packkit/`, `packs/_template/`, `test-house/packkit/`.
- Steps: commands for a person who writes a real pack without touching code (packs are data in the DSL and are never executed as code): `pack new <kind> <name>` creates the skeleton with the manifest (languages, label, sources, validity), rule stubs that require citation, label and window, and fixture stubs; `pack check <dir>` runs the validator of P06-T02, requires for every rule one fixture that fires it and one that does not, runs the reference interpreter and the generated evaluator on the fixtures and requires equal results, perturbs each threshold and each window edge by one unit and requires that at least one fixture flips (otherwise the rule is reported as under-tested), and prints the label ceiling of the pack as the weakest label among its registered sources; `pack explain <dir> <rule>` shows citation, source, label, window and the fixtures that exercise the rule; `pack diff <old> <new>` lists every outcome change and demands a new version; `pack simulate <dir>` runs the pack as an alternative activation set through the Simulation of Part 3 §3.28 on recorded SYNTHETIC cases and shows how outcomes change before any activation; `pack provenance <dir>` writes `PROVENANCE.json` (author, source ids with their registry status and retrieval month, label ceiling, version, pack hash), whose hash is the one recorded on the tenant system chain at activation. No command raises a label or sets a source status to VERIFIED: that is an owner action on the V register, and the kit only reports what is missing.
- Accept: tests T-RUL-0081 to T-RUL-0100: the skeleton validates only after its stubs are filled; a rule without a firing or a non-firing fixture is reported; a seeded under-tested threshold is reported; the interpreter and the generated evaluator agree on every kit fixture; a label above its source ceiling is refused; `pack diff` detects an outcome change without a version change; `pack simulate` leaves the case chain untouched; no kit command changes a label or a source status.

P06-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for non-determinism, evaluator divergence, window errors, silent skipping, floating-point leakage; update the registers; `reviews/P06.md`.

P06-END1, P06-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gates: `differential`, `metamorphic`, `perf`. Tier A: unit and property tests; Tier B: differential, determinism, mutation on changed modules. Test ids T-RUL-0001 to T-RUL-0100.

8. Risk review: task P06-RISK.

9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: all gates exit 0; the kernel coverage and mutation floors hold for `kernel/rules/`; `contracts.lock` includes the Pack DSL; risk review filed; README ticked by the gate.

10. Look-ahead.
- P07 calls the engine from the orchestrator through the evaluator interface only.
- Capability phases ship their packs with the generator output of this phase and the standard layout; nothing here is specific to one capability.

11. Budget: class L; retries 3; tool calls 700; token budget named in the owner's command.

<<<PHASE-07>>>
PHASE 07 — ORCHESTRATION AND CONTAINMENT

1. Header: P07 · Orchestration and containment · branch `phase/07-orchestration` · class L · capabilities: none.

2. Purpose: build the Manager, the typed agent runtime, the Tool Broker with policy as code, the containment monitors and kill switches, and the Human Gate. Agents cannot choose their goals, steps, tools or destinations; this is enforced outside them.

3. Entry conditions beyond Part 1 §8: P06 gates exit 0; the outbox, idempotency store and the deployment and tenant system chains exist; the policy engine starts in the ephemeral test stack. Evidence: the commands.

4. Scope fence. May create: `kernel/orchestrator/` (workflow DAG, Manager, budgets share), `kernel/containment/` (monitors, kill switches, autonomy levels), `agents/runtime/` (typed ports, role contract checks; no role logic of a capability), `broker/` (grants, policy decision, egress allowlist, call recording), `policies/` (Rego policies and their tests), `kernel/humangate/` (approval, rejection, override, second reviewer), `test-house/orchestration/`, `test-house/containment/`, `docs/ledger/P07.md`, risk-management records. Everything else is forbidden.
Scope paths: kernel/orchestrator/**, kernel/containment/**, kernel/humangate/**, agents/runtime/**, broker/**, policies/**, schemas/**, contracts.lock, tools/faultlab/**, tools/threat-matrix/**, tools/gate/gates/**, tools/gate/catalogue.toml, test-house/orchestration/**, test-house/containment/**, docs/ledger/P07.md, docs/phase-reports/**, docs/state/**, docs/decisions/**, risk-management/**, CHANGELOG.md, README.md

5. Context Pack: `<<<P3-S1>>>` `<<<P3-S4>>>` `<<<P3-S7>>>` `<<<P3-S9>>>` `<<<P3-S11>>>` `<<<P3-S14>>>` `<<<P3-S15>>>` `<<<P3-S25>>>` `<<<P3-S30>>>` `<<<P3-S31>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<D32-S2.3>>>` `<<<P6-S10>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P07-T01 Workflow DAG and Manager
- Files: `kernel/orchestrator/`.
- Steps: declarative workflow as signed configuration; the deterministic Manager executes the DAG, enforces step, time and retry limits, records every step on the case chain, chooses the deterministic path when one exists, and splits the case budget among roles; state transitions follow the case state machine.
- Accept: tests T-ORC-0001 to T-ORC-0014: a transition outside the DAG is rejected; a role cannot exceed its budget share; a timeout moves a case to BLOCKED and never approves; repeated delivery of one step executes once.

P07-T02 Agent runtime and role contracts
- Files: `agents/runtime/`.
- Steps: typed input and output ports per role (Part 3 §3.25), schema-validated messages, free text only inside explanation fields and never parsed for commands; the runtime refuses any output that does not match the port type; a worker failure never yields ALLOW.
- Accept: tests T-ORC-0015 to T-ORC-0026: invalid messages are rejected; a command in an explanation field has no effect; the failure of each stub role ends in ABSTAIN, BLOCKED or FAILED.

P07-T03 Tool Broker and policy as code
- Files: `broker/`, `policies/`.
- Steps: deny by default; grants name tool, scope, rate, budget, action class and readable memory classes; every call passes a Rego policy decision and is recorded with input hash, output hash and decision; the egress allowlist is data; policies carry allow and deny cases that run in CI with the policy engine's test runner.
- Accept: tests T-ORC-0027 to T-ORC-0042: an ungranted tool, an over-rate call and an off-list host are denied; a model cannot name a URL, host, path or command; every policy has allow and deny cases; each call is recorded.

P07-T04 Action classes and autonomy levels
- Files: `kernel/containment/autonomy.py`.
- Steps: classes R, P, E-rev and E-irrev; levels A0, A1, A2 per capability and tenant; default A1; any capability with an E-irrev action is capped at A2; changes are system-chain events.
- Accept: tests T-ORC-0043 to T-ORC-0050: E-irrev without a bound approval is blocked at every level; no level above A2 can be configured; defaults hold.

P07-T05 Behavior monitors and kill switches
- Files: `kernel/containment/monitors.py`, `killswitch.py`.
- Steps: monitors for step cap, loops (same tool and argument hash more than 3 times), transitions outside the DAG, budget burn rate, output size, repeated verification failures and repeated policy denials; a violation sets QUARANTINED, records Evidence, forces ESCALATE and raises an alert; kill switches pause a case, disable a capability, freeze a tenant and freeze the deployment, effective immediately and recorded on the tenant system chain or, for the deployment, on the deployment chain; a tenant freeze is lifted only by the tenant administrator and the deployment freeze only by two platform operators. Activate `containment` and `fuzz` (short).
- Accept: tests T-ORC-0051 to T-ORC-0066: each monitor fires on its seeded violation; each kill switch is exercised; a freeze rejects new commands and moves in-flight cases to BLOCKED; short fuzz of messages finds no crash.

P07-T06 Human Gate
- Files: `kernel/humangate/`.
- Steps: APPROVE bound to the exact proposal hash, REJECT and OVERRIDE with written justification as separate records that never rewrite a Decision (I11); reviewer role required; an Override of ESCALATE needs a second, different reviewer by default; approvals expire with the case state; concurrent approvals: the first wins.
- Accept: tests T-ORC-0067 to T-ORC-0078: approval of a changed proposal is invalid; an override by the same reviewer twice is refused; the Decision record is unchanged after an override; the second concurrent approval receives a conflict.

P07-T07 Adversarial containment suite and threat mapping
- Files: `test-house/containment/adversarial/`, `tools/threat-matrix/` mappings, `tools/faultlab/` registrations.
- Steps: scripted hostile instructions that try to change goals, call tools, exfiltrate, loop, exceed budgets or skip the Human Gate, run against stub roles; every case ends in quarantine, ABSTAIN or ESCALATE and never in an effect; map the tool abuse, goal drift, confused deputy and runaway items of the threat catalog; register kill points in the orchestrator.
- Accept: tests T-ORC-0079 to T-ORC-0090: 100 percent of the adversarial set ends without an effect; crash recovery of an interrupted workflow resumes without duplicate effects; mapped items have tests; `gate run containment` exit 0.

P07-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for agent autonomy, tool abuse, approval bypass, kill switch failure, budget runaway; update the registers; `reviews/P07.md`.

P07-END1, P07-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gates: `containment`, `fuzz` (short). Tier A: unit and policy tests; Tier B: containment, fuzz and crash tests. Test ids T-ORC-0001 to T-ORC-0090.

8. Risk review: task P07-RISK.

9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: all gates exit 0; every kill switch exercised in CI; policy test coverage complete; `contracts.lock` includes the workflow, port and policy schemas; risk review filed; README ticked by the gate.

- P08 adds the Model Gateway as a broker tool and the Extract stage as the only model-using role.
- P10 exposes freeze and pause through the command line and API with role gating.

Context Pack: `<<<P3-S2>>>` `<<<P3-S7>>>` `<<<P3-S8>>>` `<<<P3-S11>>>` `<<<P3-S14>>>` `<<<P3-S15>>>` `<<<P3-S16>>>` `<<<P3-S26>>>` `<<<P3-S31>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<D06-S1>>>` `<<<D06-S2>>>` `<<<D06-S3>>>` `<<<D06-S4>>>` `<<<D32-S2.2>>>` `<<<P6-S10>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>` `<<<P6-S20>>>` `<<<P6-S21>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P08-T01 Gateway interface and recorded mode
- Files: `adapters/model/gateway.py`, `recorded.py`, `schemas/model_call.json`.
- Steps: a provider-neutral interface with structured output, schema validation, per-call budget and tier; the model-call record of Part 3 §3.5 (provider, model identifier, processing region, parameters, prompt hash, response hash, response payload) written through the ledger interface; a recorded-response adapter keyed by prompt hash; the contract registry covers the interface.
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0001 to T-GWY-0012: a recorded call replays identically; an unknown prompt in recorded mode fails closed; every call writes a record; no module outside `adapters/model/` imports a provider client (architecture test).

P08-T02 Provider adapters and exit path
- Files: `adapters/model/providers/`.
- Steps: one adapter for the primary provider, one for a second commercial provider and one for a locally hosted open-weights model, all behind the same contract test suite; model identifiers pinned by exact version in configuration; adapters are exercised against local mock servers, never a live service, in CI.
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0013 to T-GWY-0024: one contract suite passes for all three adapters; switching the provider is configuration only; an identifier change without a recorded evaluation is rejected.

P08-T03 Resilience and budgets
- Files: `adapters/model/resilience.py`, `budget.py`.
- Steps: timeout, bounded retry, circuit breaker and automatic fallback to the second adapter with the fallback recorded; per-call and per-tier limits from the case budget (20 calls, 60,000 tokens, 120 s) for the meter `other`, with extraction calls drawn from the meter `extraction`; a refused call yields a recorded ABSTAIN or ESCALATE, never silent truncation; response cache keyed by input hash inside the tenant namespace, hits recorded.
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0025 to T-GWY-0036: faults at each step end in the fallback or a recorded refusal; budgets hold; a cache hit never crosses tenants; the deterministic path continues when the model is unavailable (Part 3 §3.16).

P08-T04 Extract stage
- Files: `kernel/extract/`.
- Steps: tool-less, secret-less extraction receiving only document text and the target schema; output validated against a typed schema; every field carries a locator (document id, page, span) and a method; an unextractable field becomes UNKNOWN; fields enter as PROPOSED facts. Document text is data and never becomes an instruction (I3).
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0037 to T-GWY-0050: a field without a locator is rejected; a guessed field on a document lacking it counts as a defect in the evaluation; an instruction inside a document has no effect on tools or flow.

P08-T05 Propose–Verify–Gate and grounding
- Files: `kernel/proposals/`, `kernel/extract/grounding.py`.
- Steps: proposals committed by hash before review; deterministic verifiers for schema, grounding (exact or normalized span match), rule conformance and recomputation of numbers; a failed verification rejects the proposal and is recorded, never repaired; PROPOSED facts become VERIFIED only through grounding or human confirmation; the dual-extraction hook for high-stakes fields yields HOLD on disagreement (D-06 S2).
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0051 to T-GWY-0064: a proposal changed after commitment cannot be approved; ungrounded claims are rejected; dual extraction disagreement gives HOLD; verified facts carry their locator.

P08-T06 Evaluation sets and eval gate
- Files: `evals/`, `tools/gate/gates/eval.py`.
- Steps: seeded SYNTHETIC evaluation sets per document type and language (including Arabic and Persian digit variants), an adversarial injection set, recorded responses, and baselines; metrics: field exact match, wrongly filled fields, grounding precision, injection resistance, explanation faithfulness. Activate `eval`.
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0065 to T-GWY-0076: thresholds of Part 4 §4.5 are met (exact match 95 percent, wrongly filled fields zero, grounding precision 99 percent, adversarial documents with no effect 100 percent); a seeded regression fails the gate; baselines move only through the gate.

P08-T07 Model-call tracing hooks and threat mapping
- Files: `adapters/model/tracing.py`, `tools/threat-matrix/` mappings.
- Steps: record tier, token counts and cost per call as trace attributes through the telemetry port (the exporter arrives in P10); map prompt injection through documents, exfiltration through model output and tool-authority escalation items of the threat catalog to tests; register kill points in the Gateway.
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0077 to T-GWY-0084: a recorded trace contains tier, tokens and cost; mapped items have tests; crash recovery during a call leaves exactly one call record.

P08-T08 Region profile and residency policy
- Files: `policies/region/`, `adapters/model/region.py`, `schemas/region_profile.json`.
- Steps: the RegionProfile schema (allowed storage regions, allowed model-processing regions) added through a change task; every provider adapter declares its processing region from configuration (the local adapter declares `local`); the Gateway reads the tenant's profile through a tenant-policy port (until P11 the port returns the deployment default, which is local only) and a policy-as-code rule refuses any call outside it, fails closed when the profile or the declared region is missing, and records the region in the model-call record; the refusal is an ABSTAIN with reason REGION_POLICY and a ledger event.
- Accept: tests T-GWY-0085 to T-GWY-0096: a call inside the profile succeeds and records its region; a call outside it is refused with REGION_POLICY and no provider is contacted; a missing profile allows only the local adapter; a missing declared region fails closed; the policy decision is identical for the same inputs.

P08-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for prompt injection, provider outage, silent model change, ungrounded values, cost runaway, data sent to a provider without policy; update the registers; `reviews/P08.md`.

P08-END1 also writes `docs/phase-reports/P08-viability.md` as defined in Part 6 section 6.21 (the Core Viability Review). P08-END1 and P08-END2 otherwise follow Part 1 §10.

Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: `eval` and all earlier gates exit 0; no model call exists outside the Gateway; all thresholds met on recordings; `contracts.lock` includes the Gateway interface and the model-call record; the phase report lists V-23 to V-26 as open real-data gates; risk review filed; `docs/phase-reports/P08-viability.md` exists and holds only values taken from gate outputs; README ticked by the gate.

PHASE 09 — SYNTHETIC DATA, BENCH AND WALKING SKELETON

4. Scope fence. May create: `data/synthetic/` (factory, generators, seeds, labels), `data/scenarios/` (scenario format and bench runner), `tools/bench/`, `tools/demo/` (demo harness, two-tenant scenario), `kernel/skeleton/` (the stub capability and the end-to-end driver using kernel contracts only), `tools/trace/` (traceability matrix tool), `tools/review/` (review packets and status), `test-house/review/`, `test-house/e2e/`, `test-house/synthetic/`, `docs/ledger/P09.md`, risk-management records. Everything else is forbidden.
Scope paths: data/synthetic/**, data/scenarios/**, tools/bench/**, tools/demo/**, tools/trace/**, tools/review/**, test-house/review/**, tools/threat-matrix/**, kernel/skeleton/**, evals/**, contracts.lock, tools/gate/gates/**, tools/gate/catalogue.toml, test-house/e2e/**, test-house/synthetic/**, docs/ledger/P09.md, docs/phase-reports/**, docs/state/**, docs/decisions/**, risk-management/**, CHANGELOG.md, README.md

5. Context Pack: `<<<P3-S2>>>` `<<<P3-S8>>>` `<<<P3-S10>>>` `<<<P3-S12>>>` `<<<P3-S13>>>` `<<<P3-S24>>>` `<<<P3-S26>>>` `<<<P3-S28>>>` `<<<P4-S5>>>` `<<<P4-S10>>>` `<<<P6-S6>>>` `<<<P6-S11>>>` `<<<P6-S12>>>` `<<<P2-S11>>>` `<<<P6-S21>>>`.

6. Tasks.

P09-T01 Synthetic Data Factory
- Files: `data/synthetic/`.
- Steps: a seeded, reproducible factory producing documents (including Arabic and English text, Arabic-Indic and Persian digits), facts, tariff-like tables and tenant feeds; every record is labelled SYNTHETIC with the seed and factory version; the factory is never mixed into reference data (Part 3 §3.12); output is deterministic for a fixed seed.
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0001 to T-BCH-0012: same seed gives byte-identical output; every record carries the label; no real name, company or identifier pattern from a deny list appears; the labels gate passes on factory output.

P09-T02 Scenario format and Scenario Bench
- Files: `data/scenarios/`, `tools/bench/`.
- Steps: a scenario file format with id, kind (normal, boundary, adversarial), inputs, expected outcome, a `basis` (a reference that resolves to a row of the capability tables, a rule or source id of a pack, or a requirement id) and the requirement and capability ids it covers; a bench runner that executes scenarios against the kernel and reports pass or fail by id; the format is the one Part 5 scenarios map into in later phases.
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0013 to T-BCH-0022: the runner is deterministic; an unmet expectation fails with the scenario id; a scenario without a test id is listed by the trace tool.

P09-T03 Walking skeleton, library level
- Files: `kernel/skeleton/`.
- Steps: one synthetic case runs Intake, Extract (recorded responses), Facts, Rules (a fixture pack), Decision, Evidence, Human Gate (a scripted approval) and Passport through the kernel entry points with stub capabilities; the Passport is replayed from the case bundle and verified by both the kernel verifier and the independent verifier; the skeleton uses kernel contracts only.
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0023 to T-BCH-0034: the Passport replays to an equal hash; the bundle verifies with the independent verifier; a stage failure ends in BLOCKED, ABSTAIN or ESCALATE; the skeleton imports no capability internals. Activate `e2e`.

P09-T04 Demo harness and two-tenant scenario
- Files: `tools/demo/`.
- Steps: a harness that starts the stack with seeded data and fixed timing, used later by the capture script and demonstrations; the two-tenant scenario loads two tenants and runs the same cases for both, comparing that nothing crosses.
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0035 to T-BCH-0042: the harness starts and stops cleanly; the two-tenant scenario finds zero cross-tenant reads in repositories, cache and ledger; output is identical across two runs with the same seed.

P09-T05 Traceability matrix tool
- Files: `tools/trace/`, `docs/state/` (generated matrix, never hand-edited).
- Steps: build the chain scenario, test, Definition of Done item, risk, control, test from test headers, scenario files and the risk registers; include the threat-to-test matrix of P04 and report every broken link; the matrix is attached to the phase report. Activate `trace`, which also enforces threat coverage for the items due by this phase.
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0043 to T-BCH-0054: a scenario without a test, a test without a scenario or control, a risk without a control and a control without a test each fail the gate when seeded; the generated matrix is reproducible; `gate run trace` exit 0.

P09-T06 Conformal calibration fixtures
- Files: `data/synthetic/calibration/`.
- Steps: seeded calibration and test sets with a known score distribution for the conformal core (built in P13) and the capabilities that use it; sets are versioned artifacts with an id; coverage statements using them carry the SYNTHETIC label (Part 3 §3.13).
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0055 to T-BCH-0060: sets reproduce for a fixed seed; exchangeability holds by construction (checked by a permutation test); artifact ids are stable.

P09-T07 Evaluation set replacement
- Files: `evals/`, change task P09-CHG1 if a contract changes.
- Steps: replace the small fixtures of P08 by factory output; re-record responses through the recorded-response procedure; the `eval` baselines carry over and may only improve.
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0061 to T-BCH-0066: the eval thresholds still hold on the larger set; the refreshed recording is a recorded event.

P09-T08 Scenario review packets and status
- Files: `tools/review/`, `docs/state/scenario-review-status.json` (generated), `test-house/review/`.
- Steps: `review packet <capability>` generates from the scenario files a review packet for one capability: a table of its scenarios (id, kind, inputs in readable form, expected outcome, reason codes, the basis with the text it points to) and the list of its metamorphic relations and differential references; no code, reproducible byte for byte. `review status` computes for every capability of output kind DEC (read from the registry, not from a fixed list) the hash of its canonical scenario set and compares it with the lines of the owner-only file `docs/decisions/scenario-reviews.md` (line: capability id, scenario-set hash, reviewer kind OWNER or INDEPENDENT_REVIEW, month, reference to the review report if any): REVIEWED when a line carries the current hash, CHANGED_SINCE_REVIEW when only an older hash exists, UNREVIEWED otherwise; the status file is written by the gate script only. The `trace` gate also rejects a scenario whose basis does not resolve and a review line with an unknown capability, an unknown kind or a malformed hash. No status blocks a phase. The agent never writes `scenario-reviews.md` and never sets a status by hand.
- Accept: tests T-BCH-0067 to T-BCH-0074: the packet is reproducible and contains no code; a changed scenario changes the hash and the status becomes CHANGED_SINCE_REVIEW; a capability without a line is UNREVIEWED; a line with the current hash gives REVIEWED; a malformed line or an unknown kind fails; an unresolved basis fails; the status file equals the generated one; no command writes `scenario-reviews.md`.

P09-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for synthetic data passing as real, skeleton drift, broken traceability, scenario gaps, scenarios and code that share one origin; update the registers; `reviews/P09.md`.

P09-END1, P09-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gates: `e2e`, `trace`. Tier A: unit tests of the factory and runner and the library-level skeleton; Tier B: two-tenant scenario and eval. The skeleton is part of Tier A from P10 on. Test ids T-BCH-0001 to T-BCH-0074.

8. Risk review: task P09-RISK.

9. Exit conditions beyond Part 1 §9: `e2e`, `trace` and all earlier gates exit 0; the skeleton replays and verifies with both verifiers; traceability matrix attached; `contracts.lock` consistent; README ticked by the gate; risk review filed.

10. Look-ahead.
- P10 puts the HTTP and command-line surface on the kernel; the skeleton then also runs through the API and both variants stay in Tier A.
- P13 extends the traceability tool for the support library; capability phases add each capability to the skeleton.

11. Budget: class L; retries 3; tool calls 700; token budget named in the owner's command.

6. Tasks.

P10-T01 Application factory, problem details and conventions
- Files: `api/app.py`, `api/problems.py`, `api/pagination.py`.
- Steps: versioned paths; errors as RFC 9457 problem details with the stable codes of the error registry; pagination and size limits; a foreign resource and a missing resource return the same response; the factory takes injected ports and no module-level state.
- Accept: tests T-API-0001 to T-API-0014: every registered error code maps to a problem response; a foreign id and a missing id are indistinguishable by status, body and timing class; size limits are enforced.

P10-T02 OpenAPI contract and uniform capability route
- Files: `api/routes/`, `schemas/api/`, generator extension.
- Steps: the OpenAPI 3.x document is generated from the schemas; a uniform route family through which any capability is invoked by its registration entry (invoke, status, result, evidence); every operation appears in the contract (architecture test); the interactive documentation is served. Extend `contract` with schema-driven testing and breaking-change detection.
- Accept: tests T-API-0015 to T-API-0030: every operation is in the contract; schema-driven tests find no server error on generated inputs; a seeded breaking change is reported; documentation is served.

P10-T03 Idempotency and mutating commands
- Files: `api/idempotency.py`, command routes for cases, documents, approvals.
- Steps: idempotency keys on every mutating call using the P04 store; case, document and Human Gate commands mapped to kernel services; the first approval wins and the second receives a conflict problem.
- Accept: tests T-API-0031 to T-API-0042: a repeated call returns the original result; concurrent identical calls produce one effect; stale-version writes return the conflict code.

P10-T04 Command-line interface
- Files: `cli/`.
- Steps: commands ingest, evaluate, replay, verify-ledger, prove, export and autonomy control (pause, freeze, lift) over the same services; output formats stable and machine-readable; destructive commands require an explicit flag; the skeleton also runs through the command line.
- Accept: tests T-API-0043 to T-API-0052: each command exits with documented codes; verify-ledger and prove work on a bundle with no server; autonomy commands are role-gated and ledgered.

P10-T05 Health, readiness, graceful shutdown
- Files: `api/health.py`.
- Steps: health, readiness and build-info endpoints (build identity includes the configuration hash); readiness checks storage, policy engine and the ledger signer; graceful shutdown finishes in-flight units of work.
- Accept: tests T-API-0053 to T-API-0060: readiness fails when the database is down; shutdown mid-request loses no committed work; build-info shows the configuration hash.

P10-T06 OpenTelemetry and model-call tracing
- Files: `adapters/telemetry/`, `deploy/observability/`.
- Steps: structured JSON logs with a correlation id per case, metrics and traces; tenant id is a log field and never a metric label; no personal data in logs (redaction test); model calls traced with identifier, tier, tokens and cost through the P08 hooks; the ledger remains the source of truth.
- Accept: tests T-OBS-0001 to T-OBS-0012: a metric with a tenant label is rejected by the test; seeded personal data never reaches logs; a model call yields a span with the stated attributes.

P10-T07 Canary case, objectives and reconciliation
- Files: `tools/canary/`, objectives in `deploy/observability/`.
- Steps: a synthetic canary case runs through the whole pipeline on demand and on a schedule setting (every 5 minutes, D-32 section 3); service objectives and error budgets declared as data; an automatic reconciliation compares trace records with ledger records and raises an alert on any gap.
- Accept: tests T-OBS-0013 to T-OBS-0022: the canary passes on a healthy stack and fails on a seeded fault; a deleted span is reported as a gap; objectives parse and evaluate.

P10-T08 Budget Governor
- Files: `kernel/budget/`.
- Steps: enforce the per-case defaults (20 model calls, 60,000 tokens, 120 s, 50 tool calls, 200 steps) for the meter `other` and the meter `extraction` of Part 3 §3.14 (per document page, per-script token allowance, per-document call allowance, case ceiling) and per-tenant budgets from configuration; report per meter and per document; a breach yields a recorded ABSTAIN or ESCALATE, never silent truncation; usage is exposed as events for K15 later.
- Accept: tests T-OBS-0023 to T-OBS-0032: each limit is enforced at its boundary; extraction usage never reduces the meter `other` and the reverse; the exhaustion of `extraction` leaves earlier documents intact and lists the rest as not processed; a tenant budget isolates tenants; usage events reconcile with the ledger.

P10-T09 Fault injection and threat mapping
- Files: `tools/faultlab/` registrations, `tools/threat-matrix/` mappings, `test-house/api/chaos/`.
- Steps: fault scenarios for storage unavailable, model unavailable, policy engine unavailable and slow dependencies, with the expected degraded behavior of Part 3 §3.16; map the existence-oracle, forged tenant identifier and search or aggregate endpoint items. Activate `chaos`.
- Accept: tests T-API-0061 to T-API-0072: each fault ends in the documented behavior with no partial write; mapped items have tests; `gate run chaos contract` exit 0.

P10-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for existence oracles, duplicate effects, telemetry leakage, budget bypass, test principal reaching production; update the registers; `reviews/P10.md`.

P10-END1, P10-END2: per Part 1 §10. The skeleton runs through library, command line and API.

7. Test plan. No capability. Gates extended or new: `contract` (OpenAPI), `chaos`. Tier A: unit tests, the skeleton in both variants; Tier B: schema-driven tests, chaos, canary. Test ids T-API-0001 to T-API-0072 and T-OBS-0001 to T-OBS-0032.

8. Risk review: task P10-RISK.

PHASE 11 — SECURITY AND TENANCY COMPLETION

6. Tasks.

P11-T01 Authentication and roles
- Files: `kernel/auth/`, token and session tables, API wiring.
- Steps: opaque high-entropy API tokens stored only as SHA-256 hashes with tenant, role and expiry; signed, HTTP-only, same-site session cookies for later views; roles viewer, operator, reviewer, administrator (tenant-bound) and platform operator (no tenant, deployment routes only, no tenant data access); the tenant is derived from the credential only and never from a request field; reviewer approvals separate from operator actions; the test principal of P10 is removed from production paths.
- Accept: tests T-SEC-0001 to T-SEC-0016: a token is never stored in clear; an expired or revoked token fails; a forged tenant in body, header, path and claims is ignored or rejected; each role boundary is enforced on every operation.

P11-T02 Envelope encryption and payload store
- Files: `kernel/crypto/`, `adapters/postgres/payloads/`.
- Steps: one data key per tenant wrapped by a master key held by the secret store; payloads with personal or sensitive data stored by reference and encrypted; the salts of field commitments live here; crypto-erasure by destroying a tenant data key, after which `verify --structural` passes and `verify --full` reports ERASED.
- Accept: tests T-SEC-0017 to T-SEC-0030: ciphertext differs per tenant for equal plaintext; one tenant's key cannot decrypt another's payload; after erasure payloads are unrecoverable and the ledger verifies; erasure is a recorded event.

P11-T03 Key lifecycle and secrets handling
- Files: `tools/keyctl/`, `adapters/secrets/`, `docs/key-lifecycle.md` (generated).
- Steps: documented key ceremony script, rotation (default every 90 days as configuration), break-glass procedure, key history recorded on the deployment chain, a crypto-erasure drill on a test tenant as a repeatable script; secrets read only through the port, never logged, never written to the repository; the Ed25519 signing key is held outside the kernel and the repository.
- Accept: tests T-SEC-0031 to T-SEC-0042: rotation keeps old records verifiable; the drill completes and verifies; a secret in a log line, a file or an environment dump fails the secrets tests.

P11-T04 Tenant configuration, activation and freeze
- Files: `kernel/tenancy/`.
- Steps: per-tenant budgets, rate limits, retention classes, autonomy policy and pack activation set, recorded on the tenant system chain; tenant overlays may add rules and never remove deployment rules; tenant freeze integrates with the P07 kill switches; the region profile of Part 3 §3.24 is stored with the tenant configuration, narrowing and widening follow the roles of Part 3 §3.24 and are recorded on the tenant system chain, and the tenant-policy port of P08-T08 now reads it.
- Accept: tests T-SEC-0043 to T-SEC-0052: configuration changes appear on the tenant system chain; an overlay cannot remove a deployment rule; a frozen tenant rejects commands with the documented problem code; widening a region profile without the platform operator is refused; a profile change appears on the tenant system chain; a tenant without a profile reaches no external provider.

P11-T05 Memory services
- Files: `kernel/memory/`.
- Steps: tiers M0 to M4 with the Kernel as the only writer; every write is a ledger event with provenance, label, tenant, scope, retention class and expiry; M3 precedent entries arise only from human-approved outcomes, are advisory, and aggregates require at least 5 contributing cases and carry the label DERIVED with the sample size; quarantine, tombstones and expiry job; typed queries only.
- Accept: tests T-SEC-0053 to T-SEC-0068: no write outside the write path; a model output never writes memory; M3 can never produce ALLOW; the k-threshold is enforced; expired entries leave tombstones; poisoning attempts are rejected.

P11-T06 Cross-tenant leakage suite
- Files: `test-house/leakage/`, `tools/gate/gates/leakage.py`.
- Steps: a suite that drives every API operation, repository method, cache and storage path, ledger and proof endpoint, export, log and view stub with a foreign principal and with forged identifiers, generated from the OpenAPI contract and the repository registry so new surfaces are covered automatically; includes timing-class and error-text oracles. Activate `leakage`.
- Accept: tests T-SEC-0069 to T-SEC-0084: zero leaks across all surfaces; the suite fails when a surface is added without coverage (seeded); policy tests run as the application role; `gate run leakage` exit 0.

P11-T07 Threat catalog completion
- Files: `tools/threat-matrix/` mappings, `test-house/security/`.
- Steps: map every remaining item of Part 3 §3.15 and §3.24 (supply chain items are mapped to the gates `deps`, `security`, `secrets` and `license`; hostile files to the Intake phase, P14); the tool lists the items deliberately deferred to a named later phase and fails if a deferred item names no phase.
- Accept: tests T-SEC-0085 to T-SEC-0092: no unmapped item remains except those deferred with a phase; a seeded unmapped item fails `trace`.

P11-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for tenant leakage, key compromise, token theft, role bypass, memory poisoning, erasure failure; update the registers; `reviews/P11.md`.

P11-END1, P11-END2: per Part 1 §10.

P12-T04 Backup and restore drill
- Files: `tools/ops/backup.py`, `restore_drill.py`.
- Steps: backup at the hosting platform's capability level (recorded after V-27); the restore drill restores into an ephemeral database inside the tenant's storage regions and fails if a backup or export artifact is found outside them, runs ledger verification with both verifiers and reports measured recovery point and recovery time against the objectives (15 and 60 minutes, parameters of D-32 section 3). Activate `restore`.
- Accept: tests T-OPS-0029 to T-OPS-0038: a restored database verifies; a seeded corruption of a backup is detected; the drill is repeatable and its measurements are recorded; `gate run restore` exit 0.

P12-T05 Release canary and rollback scripts
- Files: `tools/ops/canary_release.py`, `rollback.py`.
- Steps: scripts for release by canary with automatic rollback when the canary case or the error budget fails; image reference by digest; the scripts run against the local bundle in tests (no real deployment in this phase).
- Accept: tests T-OPS-0039 to T-OPS-0046: a failing canary triggers rollback to the previous digest; a healthy canary promotes; both are recorded on the deployment chain.

P12-T06 Operations threat mapping and crash points
- Files: `tools/threat-matrix/` mappings, `tools/faultlab/` registrations.
- Steps: map export, backup and log leakage and supply-chain items to tests; register kill points in backup, restore and migration steps.
- Accept: tests T-OPS-0047 to T-OPS-0052: a kill during restore leaves the target unusable-but-clean and a rerun succeeds; mapped items have tests.

P12-RISK Risk review per Part 6 §6.5: controls for failed restore, unreproducible build, secret in image, platform lock-in, failed rollback; update the registers; `reviews/P12.md`.

P12-END1, P12-END2: per Part 1 §10.

7. Test plan. No capability. New gates: `restore`, `image`. Tier A: configuration and script unit tests; Tier B: image, restore, exit drill. Test ids T-OPS-0001 to T-OPS-0052.

<<<PHASE-13>>>
PHASE 13 — CAPABILITY SUPPORT LIBRARY

1. Header: P13 · Capability support library · branch `phase/13-support-library` · class L · capabilities: none (shared modules for the capability phases).
